### Verse 7

###### Sforno on Genesis 3:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:7:1)


**וַתִּפָּקַחְנָה עֵינֵי שְׁנֵיהֶם.** נָתְנוּ לֵב אֶל כָּל עָרֵב וְתַעֲנוּג, אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁיַּזִּיק, כִּי שִׂימַת לֵב וְהַהַשְׁגָּחָה בַּדָּבָר תִּקָּרֵא פְּקִיחַת עַיִן, כְּאָמְרוֹ "אַף עַל זֶה פָּקַחְתָּ עֵינֶיךָ" (איוב יד:ג).


ותפקחנה עיני שניהם, they compared mentally, the advantages they had gained through the pleasant sensation of eating from the fruit of the tree, and the harm they had caused themselves thereby. The expression פקח implies weighing something carefully with both heart and mind. We find an example of this expression in such a sense in Job 14,3 אף על זה פקחת עינים, “Do you fix your gaze even on such a one?” [Job referring to the transient nature of man and if he is worth G’d’s attention. Ed.]


###### Sforno on Genesis 3:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:7:2)


**וַיֵּדְעוּ כִּי עֵירֻמִּים הֵם.** יָדְעוּ שֶׁרָאוּי לְכַסּוֹת מְקוֹם הָעֶרְוָה, בִּהְיוֹת מֵעַתָּה רוֹב פְּעֻלָּתוֹ מְכֻוֶּנֶת לְתַעֲנוּג מָאוּס וּמַזִּיק:


וידעו כי ערומים הם, they realized that they needed to cover their genitals which in future would serve mainly as organs designed to gratify their physical urges.



### Verse 8

###### Sforno on Genesis 3:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:8:1)


**מִתְהַלֵּךְ בַּגָּן.** אָנָה וְאָנָה כְּפִי הַתַּכְלִית הַמְכֻוָּן, כְּמוֹ "הִתְהַלֵּךְ בָּאָרֶץ" (בראשית יג:יז) וְ"יִתְהַלְּכוּ מִגּוֹי אֶל גּוֹי" (תהלים קה:יג).


מתהלך בגן, back and forth, in accordance with the requirements of their purpose. The word is similar in meaning to Genesis 13,17 where Avraham is instructed by G’d התהלך to “crisscross the land of Canaan, in order to symbolically lay claim to it.” The same construction is also found in Psalms 105,13 ויתהלכו מגוי אל גוי, “they wandered from nation to nation.”


###### Sforno on Genesis 3:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:8:2)


**לְרוּחַ הַיּוֹם.** לִרְצוֹן הַיּוֹם לַעֲשׂוֹת הַדְּבָרִים הַנִּרְצִים בְּאוֹתוֹ יוֹם, כְּמוֹ שֶׁעָשָׂה בִּשְׁאָר יְמֵי בְרֵאשִׁית, וּכְמוֹ שֶׁעָשָׂה בְּאוֹתוֹ יוֹם קֹדֶם חֶטְאָם:


לרוח היום, according to His pleasure on that day. The word רוח is used in the abstract, spiritual sense, not referring to a breeze. This is no different that what G’d had also done during the other days of creation, and as He had been doing on this same day (sixth day of creation) prior to Adam’s sin.


###### Sforno on Genesis 3:8:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:8:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:8:3)


**וַיִּתְחַבֵּא.** כְּאָמְרוֹ "וְלֹא יִרְאֶה בְךָ עֶרְוַת דָּבָר" (דברים כג:טו).


ויתחבא, as the Torah wrote in Deuteronomy 23,15 ולא יראה בך ערות דבר, “no exposure of genitals (or other shameful matters) shall be visible on you.”



### Verse 9

###### Sforno on Genesis 3:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:9:1)


**אַיֶּכָּה.** שֶׁאֵינְךָ נִרְאֶה בַּגַּן כְּמֵאָז, שֶׁנֶּחְבֵּאתָ עַתָּה וְלֹא עָשִׂיתָ כֵּן מִקּוֹדֶם?


**Where are you.** Why do you not show yourselves openly as before?



### Verse 10

###### Sforno on Genesis 3:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:10:1)


**וָאִירָא.** כְּמוֹ שֶׁקָּרָה לְיִשְׂרָאֵל אַחַר חֶטְאָם, כְּאָמְרוֹ "וַיִּירְאוּ מִגֶּשֶׁת אֵלָיו" (שמות לד:ל).


ואירא, this feeling of fear is similar to that experienced by the Jewish people in Exodus 34,30 when, because of the sin of the golden calf, the people were afraid to face him after his face radiated light when he returned from Mount Sinai.



### Verse 11

###### Sforno on Genesis 3:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:11:1)


**מִי הִגִּיד לְךָ.** יְדִיעַת הַטּוֹב וְהָרָע?


מי הגיד לך, who revealed to you the difference between good and evil?


###### Sforno on Genesis 3:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:11:2)


**כִּי עֵירֹם אָתָּה.** שֶׁבִּשְׁבִיל זֶה תִּהְיֶה רָאוּי לְהִתְכַּסּוֹת:


כי עירום אתה, that on account of familiarity with evil you felt the need to cover your genitals.



### Verse 12

###### Sforno on Genesis 3:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:12:1)


**הִיא.** שֶׁ**נָּתַתָּ** אוֹתָהּ לְעֵזֶר וּלְהוֹעִיל:


היא, she, who was meant to be an assistant to me has turned out to be the opposite.


###### Sforno on Genesis 3:12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:12:2)


**נָתְנָה לִּי מִן הָעֵץ.** וְהָיְתָה לְמִכְשׁוֹל, וּבָזֶה יִחֵס אַשְׁמָתוֹ לְקוֹנוֹ תְּמוּרַת הַתְּשׁוּבָה שֶׁהָיְתָה רְאוּיָה לוֹ, כְּמוֹ שֶׁעָשָׂה דָּוִד בְּאָמְרוֹ אֶל נָתָן "חָטָאתִי" (שמואל ב יב:יג).


נתנה לי מן העץ, and has become a hindrance instead of a help. By saying these words, Adam tried to blame his Creator for having committed a sin. Adam, instead of being defensive by repenting, apologizing, admitting his guilt, as did David when the prophet Natan had called him to order over his conduct with Bat Sheva, (Samuel II 12,13)



### Verse 13

###### Sforno on Genesis 3:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:13:1)


**מַה זֹּאת עָשִׂית.** אָמַר זֶה לְעוֹרֵר גַּם אוֹתָהּ אֶל הַתְּשׁוּבָה, כִּי לֹא יַחְפֹּץ בְּמוֹת הַמֵּת כִּי אִם בְּשׁוּבוֹ (יחזקאל יח:כג, יח:לב).


**What is this that you have done.** Hashem said this to arouse her to repent.



### Verse 14

###### Sforno on Genesis 3:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:14:1)


**אָרוּר אַתָּה מִכָּל.** שֶׁיַּשִּׂיג תַּאֲווֹתָיו וּצְרָכָיו בְּצַעַר וּבְחֶסְרוֹן תַּעֲנוּג יוֹתֵר מִכָּל שְׁאָר בַּעֲלֵי חַיִּים, כְּאָמְרָם "רָאִיתָ חַיָּה וְעוֹף שֶׁהֵם בְּנֵי אוּמָּנוּת וְהֵם מִתְפַּרְנְסִים שֶׁלֹּא בְּצַעַר" (קידושין פב:). וּפֵרֵשׁ זֶה בְּאָמְרוֹ **עַל גְּחֹנְךָ תֵלֵךְ**, שֶׁתַּשִּׂיג מְזוֹנוֹתֶיךָ בְּצַעַר, כְּאָמְרָם (ברכות נח) "שֶׁהֲרֵעוֹתִי מַעֲשַׂי וְקִפַּחְתִּי אֶת פַּרְנָסָתִי", וְאָמְרוּ כַּמָּה טְרָחוֹת טָרַח אָדָם הָרִאשׁוֹן עַד שֶׁלֹּא אָכַל פַּת.


ארור אתה מכל. The serpent was condemned henceforth to attain its needs as well as its desires only through experiencing more pain and greater lack of pleasure than all the other creatures. Compare Kidushin 82 ראית חיה ועוף שהם בני אומנות והם מתפרנסים שלא בצער, “Did you ever see a beast or bird practicing a vocation, and yet they find their livelihood without pain?” [the scholar makes the point that our parnassah lies in the hands of G’d and is not due to our professional level. Ed.] G’d’s curse is explained in detail by the words על גחונך תלך, “you will have to crawl on your belly.” This means that the serpent will find its sustenance only laboriously, experiencing frustration in doing so. Our sages (Berachot 58) mention how many laborious stages the kernel of wheat had to undergo until Adam finally was able to eat the bread that he made out of it.


###### Sforno on Genesis 3:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:14:2)


**וְעָפָר תֹּאכַל.** שֶׁלֹּא תַּשִּׂיג אֶת הַתַּעֲנוּג שֶׁהָיָה לְךָ קוֹדֶם הַחֵטְא בְּמַאֲכָל וּבְמִשְׁתֶּה וּבְמִשְׁגָּל, כְּמוֹ שֶׁקָּרָה לְיִשְׂרָאֵל בְּחֶטְאָם, כְּאָמְרָם (סוטה מט.) "הַטָּהֳרָה נָטְלָה אֶת הַטַּעַם וְאֶת הָרֵיחַ, וְהַמַּעַשְׂרוֹת נָטְלוּ אֶת שׁוּמַן הַדָּגָן":


ועפר תאכל, you will not enjoy the food (or other earthly pleasures) previously enjoyable. The Talmud Sotah 49 describes something similar happening to the Jewish people as a whole after the destruction of the Temple, saying: הטהרה נטלה את הטעם ואת הריח, והמעשרות נטלו את שומן הדגן, [the subject being the negative fallout for the entire nation of the destruction of the Temple, Ed.] “previously pleasant tasting and fragrant foods no longer tasted as well with the exception of food which needed to be consumed in a state of ritual purity, and the tithes now assumed the richness of cereals.”



### Verse 15

###### Sforno on Genesis 3:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:15:1)


**וְאֵיבָה אָשִׁית.** שֶׁתִּהְיֶה נִמְאֶסֶת בְּעֵינֵי הַכֹּחַ הַדִּמְיוֹנִי אֲפִלּוּ שֶׁלָּהּ, כְּאָמְרָם (שבת קנב.) "אִשָּׁה חֵמֶת מָלֵא צוֹאָה וּפִיהָ מָלֵא דָם". וְזֶה יְדֻמֶּה אֵצֶל הַזָּכָר וְהַנְּקֵבָה:


ואיבה אשית, woman would henceforth be an object of disgust even in her own estimation, as described in the Talmud (Shabbat 152) “although a woman is a vessel full of excrement and her orifice full of blood,” (men run after her as if she was the most desirable creature) This was going to be the relationship between males and females in the future, not only that between Adam and Chavah [who apparently could not bring himself to have intercourse with her for the next 130 years. Ed.]


###### Sforno on Genesis 3:15:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:15:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:15:2)


**וּבֵין זַרְעֲךָ וּבֵין זַרְעָהּ.** לֹא יִהְיֶה זֶה בֵּין אָדָם וְחַוָּה בִּלְבָד.


ובין זרעך ובין זרעה, not only between Adam and Chavah, but also between their respective male and female offspring.


###### Sforno on Genesis 3:15:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:15:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:15:3)


**הוּא יְשׁוּפְךָ רֹאשׁ.** הַכֹּחַ הַדִּמְיוֹנִי יְמַעֵט אֶת הַתַּעֲנוּג בִּתְחִלַּת הַשָּׂגָתוֹ, בְּמַה שֶׁיְּדַמֶּה וִיצַיֵּר יִרְאַת הֶזֵּק בְּאֵיכוּת הַתַּעֲנוּג וְכַמּוּתוֹ וְסִדְרוֹ.


הוא ישופך ראש, these thoughts crossing man’s mind will reduce the satisfaction he obtains already in the early stages of sexual intercourse, a feeling gradually reinforced when he considers the potential harm he may cause himself by indulging in copulation, diminishing both the qualitative and quantitative enjoyment of it.


###### Sforno on Genesis 3:15:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:15:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:15:4)


**וְאַתָּה תְּשׁוּפֶנּוּ עָקֵב.** כִּי הַמִּתְאַוֶּה בְּהִתְגַּבְּרוֹ יוֹלִיד הַהֶיזֵּק בְּסוֹף הַתַּעֲנוּג:


ואתה תשופנו עקב, he who initially derives pleasure from his superiority will eventually cause himself harm at the end of the pleasure cycle.



### Verse 16

###### Sforno on Genesis 3:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:16:1)


**הַרְבָּה אַרְבֶּה עִצְּבוֹנֵךְ.** דַּם הַנִּדּוֹת הַנִּקְרָא "נִדַּת דְּוֹתָהּ" (ויקרא יב:ב), כִּי אָמְנָם הִיא אָז "כָּל הַיּוֹם דָּוָה" (איכה א:יג).


הרבה ארבה עצבונך, the menstrual blood, known in the Torah and Talmud as נדת דותה (Niddah 9 and Leviticus 12,2) The prophet Jeremiah in Lamentations 1,13 describes the state of the Jewish people after the destruction of the Temple as כל היום דוה, as if she lost menstrual blood all day long.


###### Sforno on Genesis 3:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:16:2)


**וְהֵרוֹנֵךְ.** הֵפֶךְ מַה שֶׁהָיָה קוֹדֶם הַחֵטְא, כְּאָמְרָם בּוֹ בַיּוֹם נִבְרְאוּ, בּוֹ בַיּוֹם שִׁמְּשׁוּ, בּוֹ בַיּוֹם הוֹצִיאוּ תּוֹלָדוֹת (בראשית רבה כב:ב), וְכָזֹאת אָמְרוּ שֶׁתְּהִי לֶעָתִיד כְּאָמְרָם עֲתִידָה אִשָּׁה שֶׁתֵּלֵד בְּכָל יוֹם (שבת ל.), וְזֶה כִּי אָמְנָם יִשְׂרָאֵל יִהְיוּ לְרָצוֹן לִפְנֵי הָאֵל יִתְבָּרַךְ כְּמוֹ שֶׁהָיָה אָדָם הָרִאשׁוֹן קוֹדֶם חֶטְאוֹ.


והרונך, her pregnancy will be exactly the opposite of what it had been before she sinned. Our sages (Bereshit Rabbah 22,22) describe that at that time Adam and Chavah were created, copulated, and produced offspring, all on the same day. This is also what things will be like in the future according to Shabbat 30 “in the future woman will be able to give birth every day.” Such a situation will occur when the Jewish people once more regain G’d’s favour, as had been the case prior to the original sin.


###### Sforno on Genesis 3:16:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:16:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:16:3)


**בְּעֶצֶב תֵּלְדִי בָנִים.** תְּגַדְּלִי אוֹתָם בְּצַעַר יוֹתֵר מִשְּׁאָר בַּעֲלֵי חַיִּים. כִּי אָמְנָם תֵּאָמֵר מִלַּת לֵידָה עַל הַגִּדּוּל, כְּאָמְרוֹ "חֲמֵשֶׁת בְּנֵי מִיכַל בַּת שָׁאוּל אֲשֶׁר יָלְדָה לְעַדְרִיאֵל בֶּן בַּרְזִלַּי הַמְּחוֹלָתִי" (שמואל ב כא:ח).


בעצב תלדי בנים, you will experience anguish while engaged in raising them, by comparison to the other creatures on earth who do not experience such prolonged periods during which they have to look after all the needs of their young. The word לידה does appear in the context of raising children (not even one’s own) such as in the case of Michal, daughter of King Sha-ul, wife of David of whom we are told (Samuel II 21,8) חמשת בני מיכל בת שאול אשר ילדה לעדריאל בן ברזילי המחולתי“the five sons of Michal, daughter of Sha-ul whom she had born to Adriel son of Barzilai the Mecholti.” [Michal had not given birth to these five sons, but her older sister Merav, who had died very young so that Michal, her sister, had raised her nephews. Ed.]



### Verse 17

###### Sforno on Genesis 3:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:17:1)


**כִּי שָׁמַעְתָּ לְקוֹל אִשְׁתֶּךָ.** בְּיַחֲסָה לָאֵל יִתְבָּרַךְ שֶׁקֶר וְקִנְאָה:


כי שמעת לקול אשתך, by attributing to G’d such negative characteristics as lying and jealousy.


###### Sforno on Genesis 3:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:17:2)


**וַתֹּאכַל מִן הָעֵץ.** שֶׁמָּרִיתָ וְהִתְחַיַּיבְתָּ מִיתָה כְּמוֹ שֶׁהָיְתָה הַהַתְרָאָה. הִנֵּה בִּשְׁבִיל שֶׁ**שָּׁמַעְתָּ** וְקִבַּלְתָּ אֶת סְבָרָתָהּ לַחֲשׁוֹב אֶל ה' תּוֹעֶה, **אֲרוּרָה הָאֲדָמָה** שֶׁ"לֹּא תוֹסִיף תֵּת כֹּחָהּ לְךָ" (בראשית ד:יב) בִּלְתִּי עֲבוֹדָה. וּבִשְׁבִיל שֶׁמָּרִיתָ וְעָבַרְתָּ עַל מִצְוָתִי:


ותאכל מן העץ, thereby rebelling and causing yourself death as you had been warned about. Because you listened and accepted the argument to think of G’d as imperfect, ארורה האדמה, the earth will not continue to put its potential at your disposal without your having to exert yourself by hard labour. Furthermore, also because you rebelled and violated My command,



### Verse 19

###### Sforno on Genesis 3:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:19:1)


**וְאֶל עָפָר תָּשׁוּב.** כְּמוֹ שֶׁהֵעִידוֹתִי בְּךָ כְּשֶׁצִּוִּיתִי וְאָמַרְתִּי "בְּיוֹם אֲכָלְךָ מִמֶּנּוּ מוֹת תָּמוּת" (בראשית ב:יז), שֶׁתִּהְיֶה מוּכָן אֶל הַמָּוֶת בַּסּוֹף.


אל עפר תשוב, you will revert to dust, just as I had told you “on the day you eat from it you will surely become mortal.” (not that he would actually die on that same day).



### Verse 20

###### Sforno on Genesis 3:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:20:1)


**חַוָּה.** שֶׁצְּרִיכָה לִחְיוֹת וּלְגַדֵּל הַבָּנִים בְּחֶטְאָהּ:


חוה, she is a necessary requirement for the human species to continue life on earth.


###### Sforno on Genesis 3:20:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:20:2)


**כִּי הִיא הָיְתָה אֵם כָּל חָי.** אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁזֶּה יִקְרֶה גַּם לִשְׁאָר הַנָּשִׁים, מִכָּל מָקוֹם הִיא לְבַדָּהּ בָּזֶה הַשֵּׁם, כִּי הִיא הָיְתָה רִאשׁוֹנָה לְכֻלָּן.


כי היא היתה אם כל חי, even though the function of the female of the species listed here also applies to all women after Chavah, she is used as the role model and therefore accorded this name. The reason is that she was the first woman ever to fulfill this role.



### Verse 21

###### Sforno on Genesis 3:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:21:1)


**וַיַּעַשׂ כָּתְנוֹת עוֹר.** בִּלְתִּי הִשְׁתַּדְּלוּת אָדָם, כְּעִנְיָן לֶעָתִיד כְּאָמְרָם (כתובות קיא:) "עֲתִידָה אֶרֶץ יִשְׂרָאֵל שֶׁתּוֹצִיא גְּלֻסְקָאוֹת וּכְלֵי מִילַת".


ויעש ...כתנות עור, without any input by man, similar to a description of the Talmud of what the earth will produce without human input in idyllic times when ready made bread rolls will be produced by the earth (Shabbat 30)


###### Sforno on Genesis 3:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:21:2)


**וַיַּלְבִּשֵׁם.** שֶׁלֹּא יְגָרְשֵׁם עֲרֻמִּים, פֶּן בְּלָבְשָׁם אַחַר כָּךְ בְּהִשְׁתַּדְּלוּתָם יַחְשְׁבוּ שֶׁהוֹסִיפוּ מַעֲלָה:


וילבישם, in order that He would not have to expel them from Gan Eden while they were naked. Not only that, but if they would dress themselves in these coats after having been expelled, they might delude themselves that they had improved on G’d’s handiwork.



### Verse 22

###### Sforno on Genesis 3:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:22:1)


**כְּאַחַד מִמֶּנּוּ לָדַעַת טוֹב וָרָע.** הוּא יוֹדֵעַ טוֹב וָרָע עִם הֱיוֹתוֹ בְּצַלְמֵנוּ, וְאִם יִהְיֶה נִצְחִי בְּזֶה הָעִנְיָן, הִנֵּה יִרְדּוֹף כָּל הַיָּמִים אַחַר הֶעָרֵב וְיַשְׁלִיךְ אַחֲרֵי גֵוּוֹ כָּל הַשָּׂגָה שִׂכְלִית וְכָל מַעֲשֶׂה טוֹב, וְלֹא יַשִּׂיג הָאוֹשֶׁר הָרוּחָנִי הַמְכֻוָּן מֵאֵת הָאֵל יִתְבָּרַךְ בְּצַלְמוֹ וּדְמוּתוֹ:


כאחד ממנו לדעת טוב ורע, he will know good and evil even while continuing to wear “our image.” This would be an intolerable situation, as in spite of his tendency to give in to his evil urge he would live on forever. [the author considers בצלמנו as a reference to the infinite life prevailing among the celestial beings. Ed.] In such circumstances, this Adam whose evil urge was active would continue to chase the material blessings of this world, something which would prevent him from reaching the spiritual aims set for him on earth when G’d made him in the divine image.



### Verse 23

###### Sforno on Genesis 3:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:23:1)


**וַיְשַׁלְּחֵהוּ.** צִוָּה שֶׁיֵּלֵךְ מִשָּׁם, כְּמוֹ "לְמַהֵר לְשַׁלְּחָם מִן הָאָרֶץ" (שמות יב:לג).


וישלחהו, He expelled him. G’d ordered Adam to leave the garden. The expression is used in the same vein as Exodus 12,33 למהר לשלחם מן הארץ, “hastening to send them out of the country.” (Pharaoh)


###### Sforno on Genesis 3:23:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:23:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/3:23:2)


**הָאֲדָמָה אֲשֶׁר לֻקַּח מִשָּׁם.** שֶׁיִּהְיֶה הַמָּקוֹם הַהוּא וַאֲוִירוֹ נָאוֹת לְמִזְגוֹ וּמְזוֹנוֹ וּצְרָכָיו יוֹתֵר מִשְּׁאָר חֶלְקֵי הָאֲדָמָה:


האדמה אשר לוקח משם, the place to which Adam was transferred was compatible with the requirements of his constitution, more so than other areas on the globe.

**He banished the man.** Neither he nor his offspring would be permitted to return.

לשמור את דרך עץ החיים, to ensure that on the way out they would not take from its fruit and eat thereof.

ויהי הבל רועה צאן, Hevel chose this vocation as it required more intelligence and involved one’s mental activities more that farming.

וקין היה עובד אדמה, as a result of each having his own vocation, they each brought different offerings to G’d.

###### Sforno on Genesis 4:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:4:1)

**To Hevel and to his offering.** Hashem was pleased with both Hevel himself as well as with his offering.

ואל קין ואל מנחתו לא שעה, He did not turn to Kayin the donor, as he was not pleasing in the eyes of G’d; He also did not turn with favour to his offering seeing the nature of the offering was not designed to make it welcome;

ויחר, due to his jealousy of his brother,

ויפלו פניו, he was downcast, being ashamed, feeling that G’d had publicly shamed him.



### Verse 6

###### Sforno on Genesis 4:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:6:1)


**לָמָּה חָרָה לָךְ.** לָמָּה קִנֵּאתָ בְּאָחִיךָ כְּדוֹאֵג עַל שֶׁקִּבַּלְתִּי קָרְבָּנוֹ בְּרָצוֹן? הִנֵּה לֹא הָיָה זֶה רָצוֹן פָּשׁוּט אוֹ שֶׁלֹּא כַּדִּין.


**Why are you angry.** Why are you jealous that I accepted your brother’s offering? I did not do so arbitrarily nor was it unjust.

**וְלָמָּה נָפְלוּ פָנֶיךָ.** כִּי כְּשֶׁיֵּשׁ לַקִּלְקוּל אֵיזוֹ תַּקָּנָה, אֵין רָאוּי לְהִצְטַעֵר עַל מַה שֶּׁעָבַר, אֲבָל רָאוּי לְהִשְׁתַּדֵּל לְהַשִּׂיג תִּקּוּן לֶעָתִיד:


**Why are you depressed.** It is pointless to brood over the past when the matter can be rectified



### Verse 7

###### Sforno on Genesis 4:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:7:1)


**הֲלֹא אִם תֵּיטִיב.** עַצְמְךָ וְתִשְׁתַּדֵּל לִהְיוֹת גַּם אַתָּה לְרָצוֹן.


הלא אם תיטיב, if only you improve yourself you will also be welcome in My eyes.


###### Sforno on Genesis 4:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:7:2)


**שְׂאֵת.** רוּם הַמַּעֲלָה וְהַהִתְנַשֵּׂא **רֹבֵץ** לְפָנֶיךָ וּמוּכָן שֶׁיִּהְיֶה לְךָ.


שאת, every lofty level of greatness is lying in wait for you, ready to become yours,


###### Sforno on Genesis 4:7:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:7:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:7:3)


**וְאִם לֹא תֵיטִיב לַפֶּתַח חַטָּאת רֹבֵץ.** גַּם כֵּן הַחַטָּאת מוּכָן לְפָנֶיךָ, כִּי תּוֹסִיף עַל חַטָּאתְךָ פֶּשַׁע, שֶׁכָּךְ דַּרְכּוֹ שֶׁל יֵצֶר הָרָע:


ואם לא תיטיב לפתח חטאת רובץ, however, sin is also lying in wait for you if you will add further intentional sin to the already performed unintentional sin. This is the way of the evil urge, that once you give into it, it will forever be ready to tempt you again.


###### Sforno on Genesis 4:7:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:7:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:7:4)


**וְאֵלֶיךָ תְּשׁוּקָתוֹ.** כִּי תִּפְנֶה אֵלָיו לְמַלֹּאות תַּאֲווֹתָיו, כְּאָמְרָם זִכְרוֹנָם לִבְרָכָה (סוכה נב:) יִצְרוֹ שֶׁל אָדָם מִתְגַּבֵּר עָלָיו בְּכָל יוֹם.


כי אליך תשוקתו, “for it longs for you due to its very nature. It too is anxious to satisfy its desires, its aspirations every day.”


###### Sforno on Genesis 4:7:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:7:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:7:5)


**וְאַתָּה תִּמְשָׁל בּוֹ.** בְּיָדְךָ לְהִתְקוֹמֵם עָלָיו בְּצֶלֶם אֱלֹהִים, כְּאָמְרָם (סוכה נב:) וְאִלְמָלֵא הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא עוֹזְרוֹ אֵינוֹ יָכוֹל לוֹ, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (תהלים לז:לג) "ה' לֹא יַעַזְבֶנּוּ בְּיָדוֹ".


ואתה תמשול בו, it is within your power to overcome the power of the evil urge thanks to the צלם אלוקים, the divine image with which you have been provided at birth. This conforms to our sages in Sukkah 52 who have taught that were it not for this צלם אלוקים, which represents G’d’s assistance against the evil urge, there would be no way man could cope with temptation. This is what David meant in Psalms 37,33 ה' לא יעזבנו בידו ולא ירשיענו בהשפטו, “the Lord will not abandon him to his power; He will not let him be condemned in judgment.”



### Verse 8

###### Sforno on Genesis 4:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:8:1)


**וַיֹּאמֶר קַיִן אֶל הֶבֶל אָחִיו.** כִּי **חָרָה** לוֹ וְ**נָפְלוּ** פָנָיו בִּגְלַל אָחִיו.


ויאמר קין אל הבל אחיו, how upset he was, and how he had become downcast on account of his brother.


###### Sforno on Genesis 4:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:8:2)


**וַיְהִי בִּהְיוֹתָם בַּשָּׂדֶה.** שֶׁלֹּא בִּפְנֵי אֲבִיהֶם וְאִמָּם.


ויהי בהיותם בשדה, away from the presence of their parents.


###### Sforno on Genesis 4:8:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:8:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:8:3)


**וַיָּקָם קַיִן.** בְּלִי מְרִיבָה קוֹדֶמֶת אָז, כְּמוֹ "וְאָרַב לוֹ וְקָם עָלָיו" (דברים יט:יא).


ויקם קין, Kayin arose without any previous quarrel, similar to Deuteronomy 19,11 וארב לו וקם עליו, “he ambushed him and attacked him.”



### Verse 9

###### Sforno on Genesis 4:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:9:1)


**אֵי הֶבֶל אָחִיךָ.** בְּאֵיזֶה מָקוֹם קָבַרְתָּ אוֹתוֹ? וְזֶה אָמַר לְמַעַן יָשׁוּב, כִּי לֹא יַחְפֹּץ בְּמוֹת הַמֵּת.


אי הבל אחיך, “in which place did you bury him?” G’d phrased this as a question only in order to give Kayin a chance to begin the process of repentance, not because He was not perfectly aware where Kayin had buried Hevel. G’d did not want to be forced to kill someone already guilty of the death penalty.


###### Sforno on Genesis 4:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:9:2)


**לֹא יָדַעְתִּי.** חָשַׁב שֶׁהָיְתָה הַשְּׁאֵלָה מַה הָיָה לוֹ, מִפְּנֵי שֶׁלֹּא דָּרַשׁ ה' לְהַשִּׂיג נְבוּאָה אוֹ רָצוֹן כְּמוֹ קוֹדֶם לָכֵן, שֶׁחָשַׁב קַיִן שֶׁלֹּא יָדַע הָאֵל יִתְבָּרַךְ בַּפְּרָטִים זוּלָתִי בַּדְּבֵקִים בּוֹ:


לא ידעתי, “I do not know.” He thought that the question concerned Hevel’s whereabouts. Seeing that Kayin had not been interested previously in G’d’s ability to know the future, or to become close to Him; such knowledge about G’d was reserved to those who are cleaving to Him.



### Verse 11

###### Sforno on Genesis 4:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:11:1)


**אָרוּר אָתָּה מִן הָאֲדָמָה.** מְקֻלָּל וּמְחֻסָּר מִטּוֹבַת הָאֲדָמָה:


ארור אתה מן האדמה, cursed and deprived of the good the earth has to offer.


###### Sforno on Genesis 4:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:11:2)


**אֲשֶׁר פָּצְתָה אֶת פִּיהָ.** שֶׁנִּשְׁתַּמַּשְׁתָּ בָּהּ לְכַסּוֹת הֲרִיגַת אָחִיךָ, וּכְנֶגֶד זֶה לֹא תִשְׁתַּמֵּשׁ בָּהּ עוֹד כְּמוֹ שֶׁנִּשְׁתַּמַּשְׁתָּ קוֹדֶם לָכֵן לְצָרְכֵי חַיֶּיךָ. וְלֹא הֻצְרַךְ לְפָרֵשׁ עֹנֶשׁ הָרוֹצֵחַ, כִּי הַדִּין מְבוֹאָר בַּטֶּבַע עַל כָּל מַזִּיק שֶׁ"כַּאֲשֶׁר עָשָׂה כֵּן יֵעָשֶׂה לוֹ" (ויקרא כד:יט).


אשר פצתה את פיה, because it allowed itself to be used to cover up the killing of your brother. As a result, earth will no longer be free to provide the needs of your livelihood in the degree it did up until now. There was no need to spell out the penalty for the murderer. His punishment is part of the principle applying to the penalty for anyone causing willful damage, i.e. he will experience tit for tat as spelled out in Leviticus 24,19.



### Verse 13

###### Sforno on Genesis 4:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:13:1)


**גָּדוֹל עֲוֺנִי מִנְּשׂוֹא.** אַחַר שֶׁרָאָה שֶׁהָאֵל יִתְבָּרַךְ מַשְׁגִּיחַ בַּפְּרָטִים בְּהֶחְלֵט, חָשַׁב שֶׁיֵּדַע בְּלִי סָפֵק שֶׁאֵינוֹ שָׁב מֵחֶטְאוֹ בִּהְיוֹתוֹ מִתְחָרֵט אֶלָּא מִפְּנֵי הָעֹנֶשׁ, וְזֶה אַחַר הַהֶפְצֵר שֶׁהֵעִיד בּוֹ הָאֵל יִתְבָּרַךְ לָשׁוּב בִּתְשׁוּבָה וְהוּא מָרָה, וּבָזֶה אָמַר שֶׁלֹּא הָיְתָה לְחֶטְאוֹ תִּקְוַת תְּשׁוּבָה מְכַפֶּרֶת וּמְגִנָּה עַל הָעֹנֶשׁ שֶׁנֶּעֱנַשׁ עַתָּה, כְּעִנְיַן שָׁאוּל בִּדְבַר עֲמָלֵק בְּאָמְרוֹ לִשְׁמוּאֵל "חָטָאתִי" (שמואל א טו:כד) אַחַר הַהֶפְצֵר וְהַהֶתְעָרוּת שֶׁהֵעִירָהוּ שְׁמוּאֵל לָשׁוּב מֵחֶטְאָתוֹ, וְשֶׁנֶּעֱנַשׁ כָּאָמוּר "וַיִּמְאָסְךָ מִמֶּלֶךְ" (שמואל א טו:כג).


גדול עוני מנשוא, after Kayin finally realised how G’d in Heaven supervises every detail which goes on down on earth, he was convinced that G’d must know that his sole motivation in doing repentance was to escape punishment. Even this type of repentance was wrung from him only after G’d had pressured him to display penitence. He was embittered, and this is what prompted him to exclaim that the severity of his sin was such there was no hope to obtain forgiveness which would protect him against retribution in kind. The matter is similar to Saul’s failure in killing Agag, King of Amalek. Saul had said to Samuel: “I have sinned,” only after Samuel had insisted that he make a confession of having failed to carry out G’d’s instructions. (Samuel I 15,24) Samuel had had to tell Saul that G’d had despised him as a future leader of the Jewish people before he could prevail on Saul to acknowledge his wrongdoing. (Samuel I 15,24)



### Verse 14

###### Sforno on Genesis 4:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:14:1)


**וְהָיָה כָל מוֹצְאִי יַהַרְגֵנִי.** וְיִהְיֶה הָעֹנֶשׁ גָּדוֹל מְאֹד מִמַּה שֶּׁגָּזַרְתָּ:


והיה כל מוצאי יהרגני, the fact that anyone who finds me is legally entitled to kill me makes my punishment much more severe than You, G’d, have told me.



### Verse 15

###### Sforno on Genesis 4:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:15:1)


**כָּל הוֹרֵג קַיִן שִׁבְעָתַיִם יֻקָּם.** אֲנִי אוֹמֵר לְכָל מִי שֶׁהוּא מוּכָן לַהֲרֹג קַיִן, שֶׁאֲנִי גּוֹזֵר שֶׁתִּהְיֶה הַנְּקָמָה עַל חֶטְאוֹ שִׁבְעָה פְּעָמִים פַּעֲמַיִם, כִּי הַמּוּכָן לַהֲרֹג יִקָּרֵא הוֹרֵג, כְּאָמְרוֹ "וְאֶפְרַיִם לְהוֹצִיא אֶל הוֹרֵג בָּנָיו" (הושע ט:יג). וּמִלַּת שִׁבְעָתַיִם תּוֹרֶה שֶׁבַע פְּעָמִים שְׁתֵּי פְּעָמִים, כְּמוֹ "וְנִמְצָא יְשַׁלֵּם שִׁבְעָתָיִם" (משלי ו:לא). גָּזַר אִם כֵּן שֶׁעַל זֹאת הָרְצִיחָה שֶׁהָיְתָה דַּם אָחִיו וּמַחֲצִית הַמִּין הָאֱנוֹשִׁי אוֹ שְׁלִישִׁיתוֹ, תְּהִי הַנְּקָמָה כְּרוֹצֵחַ שֶׁבַע פְּעָמִים כְּפוּלִים בָּזֶה הָאוֹפֶן, שֶׁיִּהְיֶה מֶשֶׁךְ זְמַן שִׁשָּׁה דּוֹרוֹת נָע וָנָד שֶׁהֵם חַיִּים רָעִים כְּמוֹ הַמָּוֶת אוֹ יוֹתֵר מִמֶּנּוּ, כְּאָמְרוֹ "אַל תַּהַרְגֵם פֶּן יִשְׁכְּחוּ עַמִּי הֲנִיעֵמוֹ בְחֵילְךָ" (תהלים נט:יב), וּבְסוֹף יֵהָרֵג בַּדּוֹר הַשְּׁבִיעִי, כְּמוֹ שֶׁקָּרָה לוֹ עַל יְדֵי לֶמֶךְ כְּפִי מַה שֶּׁבָּא בַּקַּבָּלָה:


כל הורג קין שבעתים יוקם, I, G’d, announce that anyone who dares to kill Kayin, [i.e. who takes the law into his own hands, Ed.] would be punished seven more times than what the crime he committed appears to warrant. Anyone who is prepared to kill someone is called הורג, as for instance, in Hoseah 9,13 ואפרים להוציא אל הורג בניו, “Ephrayim too must bring out his children to his slayers.” The word שבעתים means “twice seven times,” as in Proverbs 6,31 ונמצא ישלם שבעתים, “when he is found out he must pay twice sevenfold.” G’d decreed therefore that when someone killed half the human race, or at least a third of the human race, the appropriate vengeance would involve a twice sevenfold penalty. This would manifest itself in such a slayer being a restless wanderer on earth for seven generations. Such a life is considered worse than death. When David prays in Psalms 59,12אל תהרגם פן ישכחו עמי הניעמו בחילך, “do not kill them lest my people will forget them; make them wanderers seeing it is in Your power,” he meant that they be killed only at the end of seven generations. David wanted the punishment of his enemies to last. If they would all die and disappear at once their absence would soon be forgotten. Kayin got his deserts at the end of seven generations as we know from traditional sources in Tanchuma 10.

שמען קולי נשי למך, Lemech called out in grief

אמרתי, I have to express my mental anguish’

**הָרַגְתִּי לְפִצְעִי.** חָבַלְתִּי בְּעַצְמִי מַמָּשׁ שֶׁהָיָה הֶהָרוּג אָבִי, **וְיֶלֶד לְחַבֻּרָתִי**, שֶׁהָיְתָה הַחַבּוּרָה בְּעַצְמִי מַמָּשׁ בְּמַה שֶׁהָיָה הֶהָרוּג בְּנִי.


הרגתי לפצעי, I have caused myself real injury, for the one who has killed my father (ancestor Kayin) has inflicted upon me the child a deep wound, seeing that the killer was my son.



### Verse 24

###### Sforno on Genesis 4:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:24:1)


**וְלֶמֶךְ שִׁבְעִים וְשִׁבְעָה.** שֶׁיִּהְיֶה הַצַּעַר שֶׁאֶצְטַעֵר עַל זֶה כָּל יָמַי הַרְבֵּה יוֹתֵר מִן הַצַּעַר שֶׁהִצְטַעֵר קַיִן עַל הֱיוֹתוֹ נָע וָנָד, וְזֶה כִּי הִצְטַעֵר לֶמֶךְ כָּל יָמָיו שֶׁהָרַג אֶת קַיִן זְקֵנוֹ וְתוּבַל קַיִן בְּנוֹ כְּפִי מַה שֶׁבָּא בַּקַּבָּלָה.


ולמך שבעים ושבעה. My anguish over what I have done will be many times greater than the anguish of Kayin who had been condemned to be a constant fugitive on the earth. 'כי שבעתים יוקם קין וגו, for if Kayin’s punishment was indeed great, my punishment for having killed my son will be many times greater. [he referred to the anguish he suffered over what he had done, not to the punishment imposed upon him externally, as retribution. Ed.]



### Verse 26

###### Sforno on Genesis 4:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/4:26:1)


**אָז הוּחַל לִקְרֹא בְּשֵׁם ה'.** אָז הִתְחִילוּ צַדִּיקֵי הַדּוֹר לִדְרוֹשׁ לָרַבִּים אֶת שֵׁם ה', כְּעִנְיָן "וַיִּקְרָא שָׁם בְּשֵׁם ה' אֵל עוֹלָם" (בראשית כא:לג), כִּי הֻצְרְכוּ לִסְתּוֹר דֵּעוֹת עוֹבְדֵי כּוֹכָבִים וְגִלּוּלִים שֶׁהִתְחִילוּ אָז כְּדִבְרֵי רַבּוֹתֵינוּ זִכְרוֹנָם לִבְרָכָה.


אז הוחל לקרא בשם ה, at that time the righteous people of the earth began to preach monotheism to the public. The meaning is similar to Genesis 21,33 ויקרא שם בשם ה' א-ל עולם, “Avraham proclaimed there the name of the Lord, the Lord of the universe.” The time had come to publicly confront and refute the arguments of the idolaters in that period.



## Chapter 5


### Verse 1

###### Sforno on Genesis 5:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/5:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/5:1:1)


**זֶה סֵפֶר תּוֹלְדוֹת אָדָם.** זֶה סִפּוּר תּוֹלְדוֹת וְקוֹרוֹת הַמִּין הָאֱנוֹשִׁי:


זה ספר תולדות אדם, thus begins the story of human history, and events worth recording for all posterity.


###### Sforno on Genesis 5:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/5:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/5:1:2)


**בִּדְמוּת אֱלֹהִים עָשָׂה אוֹתוֹ.** בַּעַל בְּחִירָה, לְפִיכָךְ כְּשֶׁהִכְעִיסוּ דּוֹרוֹתָיו לִפְנֵי הָאֵל יִתְבָּרַךְ נֶעֶנְשׁוּ.


בדמות אלוקים עשה אותו, a reference to man’s free will. It is in this respect that man is like G’d. This is why when different generations of man angered G’d they were punished, [i.e. they had not been programmed to do evil. Ed.]



### Verse 3

###### Sforno on Genesis 5:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/5:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/5:3:1)

ויולד כדמותו כצלמו, Sheth was more righteous than either of his brothers, seeing that even Hevel had only offered his gift to G’d after Kayin had preceded him.



### Verse 22

###### Sforno on Genesis 5:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/5:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/5:22:1)


**וַיִּתְהַלֵּךְ חֲנוֹךְ אֶת הָאֱלֹהִים.** הִתְהַלֵּךְ בִּדְרָכָיו לְהֵיטִיב לְזוּלָתוֹ בִּצְדָקָה וְתוֹכָחַת.


**Chanoch walked with Elokim.** That is, he emulated His ways by performing acts of lovingkindness and issuing rebuke.



### Verse 29

###### Sforno on Genesis 5:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/5:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/5:29:1)


**זֶה יְנַחֲמֵנוּ.** הִתְפַּלֵּל שֶׁזֶּה יְנַחֲמֵנוּ בְּהַמְצִיאוֹ מְנוּחָה מִמַּעֲשֵׂיהֶם, כִּי מִלַּת נֹחַ תּוֹרֶה מְנוּחָה, כְּמוֹ "וְנוֹחַ מֵאוֹיְבֵיהֶם" (אסתר ט:טז).


זה ינחמנו, Lemech prayed that this son would provide him with the ability to take a rest from his heavy labour. The root of the word נח is מנוחה, rest, (retirement). The word is also used in connection with giving one a respite from one’s enemies, as in Esther 9,16.



## Chapter 6


### Verse 3

###### Sforno on Genesis 6:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:3:1)


**לֹא יָדוֹן רוּחִי בָאָדָם לְעוֹלָם בְּשַׁגַּם הוּא בָשָׂר.** אֵינוֹ רָאוּי שֶׁלְּעוֹלָם יִהְיֶה מָדוֹן לְפָנַי וּפִתְחוֹן פֶּה לַחֲלוֹק וְלוֹמַר, שֶׁאַף עַל פִּי שֶׁהָאָדָם רָאוּי לְעוֹנֶשׁ רַב בְּמִרְיוֹ בִּשְׁבִיל הֱיוֹתוֹ בְּצֶלֶם וּבִדְמוּת, מִכָּל מָקוֹם רָאוּי גַּם כֵּן לְרַחֵם עָלָיו בִּשְׁבִיל שֶׁגַּם כֵּן הוּא בָּשָׂר מַשִּׂיא לַחֲטוֹא, לֹא צֶלֶם וּדְמוּת בִּלְבָד:


לא ידון רוחי באדם לעולם בשגם הוא בשר, it is intolerable that there will continue the argument, strife before Me allowing (the angels?) to claim that although seeing that man has been endowed with divine qualities he must be punished, whereas his close ties to physical earth must be considered as excuses for his conduct, entitling him to continued mercy, and indulgence by Me.


###### Sforno on Genesis 6:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:3:2)


**וְהָיוּ יָמָיו.** יִהְיֶה לוֹ זְמַן לִתְשׁוּבָה:


והיו ימיו, he will be given a certain length of time within which to repent his sins;


###### Sforno on Genesis 6:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:3:3)


**מֵאָה וְעֶשְׂרִים שָׁנָה.** שֶׁבֵּין כָּךְ הָיָה נֹחַ עוֹשֶׂה הַתֵּבָה וּמוֹכִיחַ אוֹתָם וּמַתְרֶה בָּם, כְּדִבְרֵי רַבּוֹתֵינוּ זִכְרוֹנָם לִבְרָכָה (סנהדרין קח:).


מאה ועשרים שנה, during which time Noach would have time to construct the ark, and by doing so rebuking and warning people of what is in store for them. (compare Sanhedrin 108)



### Verse 4

###### Sforno on Genesis 6:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:4:1)


**הַנְּפִילִים הָיוּ בָאָרֶץ בַּיָּמִים הָהֵם.** שֶׁקָּבַע לָהֶם הָאֵל יִתְבָּרַךְ הַזְּמַן לִתְשׁוּבָה, וְגַם אַחֲרֵי כֵן שֶׁלֹּא שָׁבוּ כְּלָל.


הנפילים היו בארץ בימים ההם, these giants were not about until the last 120 years which G’d had assigned man as a grace period within which to do teshuvah וגם אחר כן, as well as thereafter, i.e. they did not repent and mend their ways at all.



### Verse 5

###### Sforno on Genesis 6:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:5:1)


**כִּי רַבָּה רָעַת הָאָדָם.** לְשֶׁעָבַר.


כי רבה רעת האדם, a reference to the past,


###### Sforno on Genesis 6:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:5:2)


**וְכָל יֵצֶר מַחְשְׁבוֹת לִבּוֹ.** לֶעָתִיד שֶׁלֹּא הָיוּ שׁוֹמְעִים לַמּוֹכִיחַ וְאֵין לִקְווֹת שֶׁיָּשׁוּבוּ:


וכל יצר לב האדם, a reference to the future (when it would not improve)



### Verse 6

###### Sforno on Genesis 6:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:6:1)


**וַיִּתְעַצֵּב אֶל לִבּוֹ.** כִּי לֹא יַחְפֹּץ בְּמוֹת הַמֵּת, וְהַהֵפֶךְ "יִשְׂמַח ה' בְּמַעֲשָׂיו" (תהלים קד:לא).


ויתעצב אל לבו, seeing that G’d does not want even the guilty to die instead of finding their way back. The opposite “emotion” of G’d to the one expressed here is found in Psalms 104,31 ישמח ה' במעשיו, “the Lord ‘enjoys’ the deeds of His creatures.”



### Verse 8

###### Sforno on Genesis 6:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:8:1)


**וְנֹחַ מָצָא חֵן.** לְהַצִּיל גַּם בָּנָיו וּבְנוֹתָיו לֹא מִפְּנֵי שֶׁהָיָה רָאוּי לְכָךְ, אֲבָל עַל צַד חֲנִינָה זִכָּהוּ הָאֵל יִתְבָּרַךְ לָזֶה, כְּאָמְרוֹ: "וּשְׁלֹשֶׁת הָאֲנָשִׁים הָאֵלֶּה בְּתוֹכָהּ נֹחַ דָּנִיֵּאל וְאִיּוֹב חַי ה' אִם בָּנִים וְאִם בָּנוֹת יַצִּילוּ הֵמָּה לְבַדָּם יִנָּצֵלוּ" (יחזקאל יד:יד). וְזֶה כִּי לֹא לִמְּדוּ אֶת דּוֹרָם לָדַעַת אֶת ה' כְּאַבְרָהָם מֹשֶׁה וּשְׁמוּאֵל וְזוּלָתָם, כְּאָמְרָם זִכְרוֹנָם לִבְרָכָה: "עֵילָם וְהֵם דָּנִיֵּאל וַחֲבֵרָיו זָכְתָה לִלְמוֹד וְלֹא זָכְתָה לְלַמֵּד" (פסחים פז:). וְכֵן נֹחַ, אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁהוֹכִיחַ עַל הַמַּעֲשִׂים הַמְקֻלְקָלִים עִנְיַן הַמְּדִינוֹת, לֹא הוֹרָה אוֹתָם לָדַעַת הָאֵל יִתְבָּרַךְ וְלָלֶכֶת בִּדְרָכָיו, אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁהוּא הָיָה צַדִּיק תָּמִים בָּעִיּוּן וּבַמַּעֲשֶׂה. כִּי אָמְנָם צַדִּיק הַמַּשְׁלִים עַצְמוֹ בִּלְבַד הוּא רָאוּי שֶׁיִּמָּלֵט עַצְמוֹ בִּלְבַד, אֲבָל הַמַּשְׁלִים גַּם אֶת זוּלָתוֹ הוּא רָאוּי שֶׁיִּמָּלֵט גַּם אֶת זוּלָתוֹ, כִּי בָּזֶה יֵשׁ תִּקְוָה שֶׁיַּחֲזִירֵם בִּתְשׁוּבָה, כְּאָמְרָם זִכְרוֹנָם לִבְרָכָה (ברכות יט.): "אִם רָאִיתָ תַּלְמִיד חָכָם שֶׁסָּרַח בַּיּוֹם אַל תְּהַרְהֵר אַחֲרָיו בַּלַּיְלָה, שֶׁוַּדַּאי עָשָׂה תְּשׁוּבָה". חסלת פרשת בראשית.


**But Noach found favor (*chen*).** *Chen* implies groundless favor. Because Noach did not actively spread knowledge of Hashem he was not worthy that others should be saved on his account. Thus it was an act of groundless favor to him that his sons and daughters-in-law were spared.



### Verse 9

###### Sforno on Genesis 6:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:9:1)


**אֵלֶּה תּוֹלְדוֹת נֹחַ.** עִנְיָנָיו וְיַלְדֵי יָמָיו.


תולדות נח, a reference to his history, commencing with his own life and continuing with that of his offspring.


###### Sforno on Genesis 6:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:9:2)


**צַדִּיק.** בְּמַעֲשֶׂה.


צדיק, in his deeds,


###### Sforno on Genesis 6:9:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:9:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:9:3)


**תָּמִים.** בַּמֻשְׂכָּלוֹת.


תמים, in his attitudes,


###### Sforno on Genesis 6:9:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:9:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:9:4)


**בְּדוֹרוֹתָיו.** כְּפִי דּוֹרוֹתָיו, וְהֵם הָיוּ קְצָת דּוֹרוֹ שֶׁל מְתוּשֶׁלַח וְשֶׁל לֶמֶךְ וְשֶׁלּוֹ, וְהַדּוֹרוֹת שֶׁהוֹלִידוּ בְּנֵי דּוֹרוֹ בְּאַרְבַּע מֵאוֹת וְעֶשְׂרִים שָׁנָה:


בדורותיו, according to the generations during which he lived. They included part of the generation of Metushelach, of Lemech, and those during the 600 years preceding the deluge.


###### Sforno on Genesis 6:9:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:9:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:9:5)


**אֶת הָאֱלֹהִים הִתְהַלֶּךְ נֹחַ.** הָלַךְ בִּדְרָכָיו לְהֵיטִיב לְזוּלָתוֹ, וְהוֹכִיחַ בְּנֵי דּוֹרוֹ כְּדִבְרֵי רַבּוֹתֵינוּ זִכְרוֹנָם לִבְרָכָה. וְכֵן כָּתַב עָלָיו בְּרוּס הַכַּלְדִּיִּי:


את האלוקים התהלך נח, he walked in G’d’s way trying to be helpful to others, and to instruct and if necessary to rebuke them, as our sages pointed out. This is also in accordance with what the historian Berussi Hacaldaii (3rd century B.C.) wrote about him.



### Verse 10

###### Sforno on Genesis 6:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:10:1)


**וַיּוֹלֶד נֹחַ.** מֵאָז שֶׁהִתְחִיל לְהוֹכִיחַ בְּנֵי דּוֹרוֹ זָכָה לְבָנִים.


ויולד נח, as soon as he began to rebuke his fellowmen he was granted children



### Verse 12

###### Sforno on Genesis 6:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:12:1)


**וְהִנֵּה נִשְׁחָתָה.** מֵעַצְמָהּ, כִּי בְּזוּלַת עֹנֶשׁ הָיְתָה בְּדֶרֶךְ הַשְׁחָתָה בְּהַשְׁחָתַת דַּרְכָּם הַמְקַלְקֶלֶת הַתּוֹלָדָה וּבַגָּזֵל הַמְקַלְקֵל הַמְּדִינוֹת, כְּעִנְיַן "וְטַחֲנִי קָמַח" (ישעיהו מז:ב).


והנה נשחתה, without external influences, as a natural consequence, not due to man’s ecologically criminal behaviour. Although on the surface, there appeared to be no connection, man’s corruption resulted in the corruption of his habitat. The language used here by the Torah is comparable to Isaiah 47,2 וטחני קמח, “and grind meal.” One does not grind flour, but one grinds grain into flour. The prophet spoke about the end product. The Torah also spoke about the end product of man’s corruption bringing in its wake, though invisibly, the destruction of the earth’s crust.



### Verse 13

###### Sforno on Genesis 6:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:13:1)


**קֵץ כָּל בָּשָׂר.** שֶׁקָּצַבְתִּי לָהֶם זְמַן מֵאָה וְעֶשְׂרִים שָׁנָה.


**The end of all flesh.** That is, the end of their 120 year reprieve.


###### Sforno on Genesis 6:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:13:2)


**בָּא לְפָנַי.** שֶׁקָּבַעְתִּי לָהֶם לִתְשׁוּבָה.


בא לפני, this period has now expired.


###### Sforno on Genesis 6:13:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:13:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:13:3)


**כִּי מָלְאָה הָאָרֶץ חָמָס מִפְּנֵיהֶם.** שֶׁכֻּלָּם גּוֹזְלִים זֶה אֶת זֶה. הַבְּעָלִים גּוֹזְלִים אֶת הָאָרִיס בְּכֹחַ, וְהָאָרִיס גּוֹזֵל אֶת הַבְּעָלִים בְּמִרְמָה, בְּאֹפֶן שֶׁהָאָרֶץ נוֹתֶנֶת כָּל פֵּרוֹתֶיהָ לַגַּזְלָנִים:


**The earth is filled with violent crime.** A landlord would steal openly from his tenant farmer while the tenant farmer stole surreptitiously from the landlord in return. Thus all the earth’s bounty went to thieves.


###### Sforno on Genesis 6:13:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:13:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:13:4)


**וְהִנְנִי מַשְׁחִיתָם אֶת הָאָרֶץ.** אַשְׁחִיתֵם יַחְדָּיו עִם הָאָרֶץ, שֶׁאַשְׁחִית מֶזֶג הָאָרֶץ וְהָאֲוִיר בִּנְטִיַּת גַּלְגַּל חַמָּה שֶׁהִטָּה מִמַּשְׁוֶה הַיּוֹם מִן הַמַּבּוּל וְאֵילָךְ, כְּמוֹ שֶׁפֵּרֵשׁ הוּא יִתְבָּרַךְ בְּמַעֲנֵהוּ לְאִיּוֹב (איוב לח:ד-טו), וְלָכֵן נִמְעֲטוּ שְׁנֵי חַיֵּי הַמִּין הָאֱנוֹשִׁי תֵּכֶף אַחַר הַמַּבּוּל, כִּי לֹא הָיוּ עוֹד הַמְּזָגִים וְהַפֵּרוֹת עַל שְׁלֵמוּתָם הָרִאשׁוֹן. וְלָזֶה הוּתַּר לַמִּין הָאֱנוֹשִׁי אֲכִילַת בַּעֲלֵי חַיִּים אַחַר הַמַּבּוּל:


והנני משחיתם את הארץ, so that now I will destroy them together with the earth. I will destroy the climate which could support life on earth by interfering with the sun’s orbit and rearranging it from the beginning of the deluge for the entire future. We find that G’d explained this to Job In Job 38, 4-15. This accounts for the lifespan of man having been drastically reduced after the deluge. The climate of the earth changed, there were greater extremes of heat and cold, the produce of the earth was considerably less capable of supporting a long lifespan. As a by-product of this deterioration in the quality of the vegetable products, man was allowed to eat meat as a compensation.

**עֲשֵׂה לְךָ תֵּבַת.** בַּזְּמַן הַקָּצוּב לָהֶם לְמַעַן יִרְאוּ וְיָשׁוּבוּ:


עשה לך תבת וגו, during the period allocated to them, in order to remind them to do teshuvah.



### Verse 16

###### Sforno on Genesis 6:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:16:1)


**בְּצִדָּהּ תָּשִׂים.** בְּצַד רָחְבָּהּ, כִּי הוּא יִקָּרֵא צַד, וּפְאַת הָאֹרֶךְ יִקָּרֵא צֶלַע.


בצדה תשים, on the side which is wide, for that is the side called צד. The long side is referred to as צלע.


###### Sforno on Genesis 6:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:16:2)


**תַּחְתִּיִּם.** הַמּוּרְגָּלִים בָּאֳנִיּוֹת.


תחתיים, the lower deck in ships; as in any other ship, the customary lower deck.


###### Sforno on Genesis 6:16:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:16:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:16:3)


**שְׁנִיִּים וּשְׁלִישִׁים.** דּוֹמִים לַתַּחְתִּיִּים הַמֻּרְגָּלִים:


שניים ושלשים, they are similar, parallel to the lower deck.



### Verse 17

###### Sforno on Genesis 6:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:17:1)


**וַאֲנִי הִנְנִי מֵבִיא אֶת הַמַּבּוּל.** אַתָּה תַּשְׁלִים הַתֵּיבָה וַאֲנִי תֵּכֶף אָבִיא אֶת הַמַּבּוּל, וְהוּא לְשׁוֹן מַפָּלָה וְהֶפְסֵד כְּעִנְיַן "כָל נְבֵלָה" (דברים יד:כא). אָמַר אֲנִי אָבִיא אֶת הַמַּפָּלָה וְהַהֶפְסֵד שֶׁאָמַרְתִּי בְּאָמְרִי **וְהִנְנִי מַשְׁחִיתָם אֶת הָאָרֶץ**:


ואני הנני מביא את המבול, G’d is saying, that Noach’s task is to build the ark, whereas He, when the time expires, will bring on the deluge He had threatened as soon as the ark would be completed. The word מבול is a variant of מפלה, referring to something lost as in Deut. 14,21 כל נבלה, a carcass being a life lost. The definitive article ה in המבול refers to the destruction, the nature of which had not previously been spelled out. [my paraphrasing. Ed.]



### Verse 18

###### Sforno on Genesis 6:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:18:1)


**וַהֲקִמֹתִי אֶת בְּרִיתִי.** אַחַר הַמַּבּוּל.


והקימותי את בריתי, a reference to the covenant G’d made with Noach after the deluge.



### Verse 21

###### Sforno on Genesis 6:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/6:21:1)


**וְאַתָּה.** עַתָּה קַח מִכָּל מַאֲכָל, מַאֲכָלִים שׁוֹנִים לְמִינִים שׁוֹנִים.


ואתה קח לך מכל מאכל, different menus for different species of creatures.



## Chapter 7


### Verse 1

###### Sforno on Genesis 7:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/7:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/7:1:1)


**כִּי אוֹתְךָ רָאִיתִי צַדִּיק.** לֹא בֵּיתְךָ, לְפִיכָךְ **אַתָּה וְכָל בֵּיתְךָ**, כִּי בִּשְׁבִילְךָ בִּלְבַד אֲמַלְּטֵם:


כי אותך ראיתי צדיק, not your family, therefore, אתה וכל ביתך, I will let them escape only on account of your merit.



### Verse 2

###### Sforno on Genesis 7:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/7:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/7:2:1)


**הַטְּהוֹרָה.** שֶׁהָיוּ אָז כֻּלָּם כְּשֵׁרִים לְהַקְרָבָה, כְּמוֹ שֶׁהִזְכִּירוּ רַבּוֹתֵינוּ זִכְרוֹנָם לִבְרָכָה, וּכְמוֹ כֵן בִּסְגֻלַּת מְזוֹנָהּ עַל הֵפֶךְ "וְנִטְמֵתֶם בָּם".


הטהורה, at that time all of the pure animals [including free roaming ones] were acceptable as potential offerings to G’d.



### Verse 18

###### Sforno on Genesis 7:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/7:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/7:18:1)


**וַתֵּלֶךְ הַתֵּיבָה.** בִּדְחִיַּת הַתִּגְבּוֹרֶת הַבָּאָה מִלְמַטָּה.


ותלך התבה, not under its own power but by the pressure of the external waters.



### Verse 23

###### Sforno on Genesis 7:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/7:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/7:23:1)


**וַיִּמַח אֶת כָּל הַיְקוּם.** שֶׁל בַּעֲלֵי חַיִּים, לֹא עֲשָׂבִים וּצְמָחִים, כְּמוֹ שֶׁבֵּאֵר בְּאָמְרוֹ **מֵאָדָם** וְ**עַד בְּהֵמָה עַד רֶמֶשׂ וְעַד עוֹף הַשָּׁמַיִם**:


וימח את כל היקום, the structure of all the living beings was completely dissolved, but not that of the plants. “[trunks of trees might survive in recognisable form, whereas bodies would not be recognisable. Ed.]


###### Sforno on Genesis 7:23:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/7:23:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/7:23:2)


**וַיִּמָּחוּ מִן הָאָרֶץ.** שֵׁם וּשְׁאָר וְנִין וְנֶכֶד.


This is why the Torah added the words מאדם ועד בהמה, עד רמש ועד עוף השמים וימחו מן הארץ, “man or beast, moving creatures, including the birds of the sky disintegrated completely.” No traceable remains survived.



### Verse 24

###### Sforno on Genesis 7:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/7:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/7:24:1)


**וַיִּגְבְּרוּ כּוּ' חֲמִשִּׁים וּמְאַת יוֹם.** מִיּוֹם הַתְחָלַת הַגֶּשֶׁם גָּבְרוּ עַד תֹּם חֲמִשִּׁים וּמְאַת יוֹם. מִתְּחִלַּת הַגֶּשֶׁם שֶׁהָיָה בְּשִׁבְעָה עָשָׂר לַחֹדֶשׁ הַשֵּׁנִי וְשָׁלְמוּ בְּשִׁבְעָה עָשָׂר לַחֹדֶשׁ הַשְּׁבִיעִי שֶׁהָיוּ הַמֵּאָה וַחֲמִשִּׁים יוֹם חֲמִשָּׁה חֳדָשִׁים שְׁלֵמִים, וְאָז נָחָה הַתֵּבָה שֶׁלֹּא הָיָה שׁוּם תִּגְבֹּרֶת דּוֹחֶה. כִּי אָמְנָם מִיּוֹם הַתְחָלַת הַגֶּשֶׁם הִתְחִיל הַתִּגְבֹּרֶת מִלְּמַטָּה, כְּאָמְרוֹ **נִבְקְעוּ כָּל מַעְיְנוֹת תְּהוֹם רַבָּה**. אָמְנָם לֹא הִתְחִילָה הַתֵּבָה לָלֶכֶת עַל פְּנֵי הַמַּיִם עַד שֶׁהָיוּ הַמַּיִם כָּל כָּךְ גְּבוֹהִים שֶׁהַתֵּבָה רָמָה מֵעַל הָאָרֶץ, וְכַאֲשֶׁר שָׁלְמוּ יְמֵי הַגֶּשֶׁם הָיָה תִּגְבֹּרֶת מִלְּמַטָּה בִּלְבַד נִמְשָׁךְ עַד מְלֹאת מֵאָה וַחֲמִשִּׁים יוֹם, וְאָז בְּנוּחַ הַתִּגְבֹּרֶת הַדּוֹחֶה אֶת הַתֵּבָה נָחָה הַתֵּבָה.


ויגברו...חמישים ומאת יום. These 150 days are counted from the beginning of the 40 days of rainfall. The level of water on the surface of the earth kept increasing for 150 days, concluding on the 17th of the seventh month (Nissan). On that date the ark came to rest (on the waters) seeing there was no pressure exerted on it from any direction. The pressure on the ark to move was exerted from the first day of the rain when the Torah simultaneously reported the fountains below opening and adding their water to that of the rain. (verse 11). The ark resisted this pressure until the waters on earth were so high that they lifted the ark from its base. When the days of rainfall had concluded, the pressure from the subterranean waters kept up until the end of 150 days. When that pressure ceased the ark finally came to rest on Mount Ararat.

### Verse 7

**וַיְשַׁלַּח אֶת הָעוֹרֵב.** לִרְאוֹת אִם יָבֵשׁ הָאֲוִיר אַחַר שֶׁנִּרְאוּ רָאשֵׁי הֶהָרִים, בְּאֹפֶן שֶׁיּוּכַל הָעוֹרֵב לְסָבְלוֹ.


וישלח את העורב, to find out if the atmosphere had dried out after the tops of the mountains had become visible. Noach wanted to know if the atmosphere in the meanwhile was such that the raven could tolerate it.

**וַיֵּצֵא יָצוֹא וָשׁוֹב.** שֶׁלֹּא יָכוֹל לְסָבְלוֹ:


ויצא יצוא ושוב, this proved that the atmosphere was not yet dry enough for the raven to tolerate it for any extended period of time.

וישלח את היונה, all of the seven pairs of pigeons [based on the latter ה in front of the word יונה. Ed.]

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/8:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/8:8:2)


**לִרְאוֹת הֲקַלּוּ.** שֶׁאִם הָקַלּוּ תְּקַנֵּן בֶּהָרִים וּבַמִּגְדָּלִים כְּמִנְהָגָהּ.


לראות הקלו, if the atmosphere had improved they would build their nests in the mountains and high structures as is their custom.

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/8:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/8:9:1)


**כִּי מַיִם עַל פְּנֵי כָל הָאָרֶץ.** גַּם בְּרָאשֵׁי הֶהָרִים הַנִּרְאִים הָיָה לַחוּת רַב, בְּאֹפֶן שֶׁגַּם שָׁם **לֹא מָצְאָה מָנוֹחַ לְכַף רַגְלָהּ**.


כי מים על פני כל הארץ, even on the tops of the mountains which had become visible, everything was still thoroughly wet so that even there לא מצא מנוח לכף רגלו, it could not find a resting place for its foot.

**וַיָּסַר נֹחַ אֶת מִכְסֵה הַתֵּבָה.** חָשַׁב שֶׁכְּבָר יָבֵשׁ לַחוּת הָאֲוִיר הַמּוֹתְרִיי:


ויסר נח את מכסה התבה, he had thought that the earth had already dried out completely only to find


###### Sforno on Genesis 8:13:2

והנה חרבה פני האדמה, that whereas there was a thin layer of earth above the water, the earth had not yet dried out thoroughly.

###### Sforno on Genesis 8:21:1

את ריח הניחוח, proving that at that time all these species of ritually pure animals were fit as sacrifices.


###### Sforno on Genesis 8:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/8:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/8:21:2)


**וַיֹּאמֶר ה' אֶל לִבּוֹ.** שֶׁלֹּא גִּלָּה לְנֹחַ וּלְבָנָיו עַד שֶׁקִּבְּלוּ מִצְווֹתָיו וְהֵקִים בְּרִית.


**In His heart.** He did not reveal this resolution to Noach and his sons until after they had accepted His commandments and He established His covenant with them.


###### Sforno on Genesis 8:21:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/8:21:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/8:21:3)


**כִּי יֵצֶר לֵב הָאָדָם רַע.** כִּי בִּהְיוֹת הַמְּזָגִים מִכָּאן וָאֵילָךְ פְּחוּתִים מֵאֲשֶׁר הָיוּ קֹדֶם הַמַּבּוּל, לֹא יָאִיר בָּהֶם הַכֹּחַ הַשִּׂכְלִי מִנְּעוּרֵיהֶם כְּבָרִאשׁוֹנָה, בְּאֹפֶן שֶׁיִּתְקוֹמֵם לַמִּתְאַוֶּה הַמִּתְגַּבֵּר עָלָיו **מִנְּעוּרָיו.**


**For the inclination of man’s heart.** Human makeup was now inferior to what it was before the flood and the intellect no longer held sway during the years of youth. This enabled the appetites to achieve domination



### Verse 22

###### Sforno on Genesis 8:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/8:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/8:22:1)


**עוֹד כָּל יְמֵי הָאָרֶץ זֶרַע וְקָצִיר וְקֹר וָחֹם וְקַיִץ וָחֹרֶף וְיוֹם וָלַיְלָה לֹא יִשְׁבֹּתוּ.** לֹא יִשְׁבֹּתוּ מִלְּהַתְמִיד עַל אוֹתוֹ הָאֹפֶן בִּלְתִּי טִבְעִי שֶׁהִגְבַּלְתִּי לָהֶם אַחַר הַמַּבּוּל. וְזֶה שֶׁיֵּלֵךְ הַשֶּׁמֶשׁ עַל גַּלְגַּל נוֹטֶה מִקַּו מַשְׁוֶה הַיּוֹם, וּבִנְטִיָּתוֹ תִּהְיֶה סִבַּת הִשְׁתַּנּוּת כָּל אֵלֶּה הַזְּמַנִּים, כִּי קֹדֶם הַמַּבּוּל הָיָה מַהֲלַךְ הַשֶּׁמֶשׁ תָּמִיד בְּקַו מַשְׁוֶה הַיּוֹם, וּבָזֶה הָיָה אָז תָּמִיד עֵת הָאָבִיב, וּבוֹ הָיָה תִּקּוּן כְּלָלִי לַיְסוֹדוֹת וְלַצְּמָחִים וּלְבַעֲלֵי חַיִּים וּלְאֹרֶךְ יְמֵיהֶם. וְאָמַר שֶׁיִּהְיֶה זֶה כָּל יְמֵי הָאָרֶץ עַד אֲשֶׁר יְתַקֵּן הָאֵל יִתְבָּרַךְ אֶת הַקִּלְקוּל שֶׁנַּעֲשָׂה בָּהּ בַּמַּבּוּל, כְּאָמְרוֹ (ישעיהו סו:כב) "הָאָרֶץ הַחֲדָשָׁה אֲשֶׁר אֲנִי עוֹשֶׂה", כִּי אָז יָשִׁיב מַהֲלַךְ הַשֶּׁמֶשׁ אֶל קַו מַשְׁוֶה הַיּוֹם כְּמֵאָז, וְיִהְיֶה תִּקּוּן כְּלָלִי לַיְסוֹדוֹת וְלַצְּמָחִים וּלְבַעֲלֵי חַיִּים וּלְאֹרֶךְ יְמֵיהֶם כְּמוֹ שֶׁהָיָה קֹדֶם הַמַּבּוּל, כְּאָמְרוֹ "כִּי הַנַּעַר בֶּן מֵאָה שָׁנָה יָמוּת וְהַחוֹטֵא בֶּן מֵאָה שָׁנָה יְקֻלָּל", וְזֶה רָצָה בְּאָמְרוֹ (תהלים סה:ט) "מוֹצָאֵי בֹקֶר וָעֶרֶב תַּרְנִין".


עוד כל ימי הארץ זרע וקציר, וקור וחום, וקיץ וחורף, ויום ולילה, לא ישבתו; nature will not cease to function dependably as it had prior to the deluge. The sun will orbit predictably, its orbit will be subject to calculation in advance, so will be its position relative to other phenomena in the sky. All the time periods mentioned in this verse will somehow be related to the position of the sun at different times during the year. It would be different from before the deluge. Whereas prior to the deluge the sun had remained at a fixed distance relative to the earth all year round, its orbit being circular, resulting in eternal spring in the populated parts of the earth, now there would be all these changes, though at predetermined intervals, resulting in alternating seasons of relative warmth and cold due to the elliptical nature of the sun’s orbit [Although the author lived approximately at the time of Copernicus having determined that the earth moves around the sun instead of being the center of our galaxy, the basic concept even according to the ancient world view does not invalidate the author’s description of antediluvian and postdiluvian climatic conditions. Ed.. כל ימי הארץ, until such time as G’d would correct this deterioration in living conditions on earth due to the effect of the deluge. This “correction” is what the prophet Isaiah 66,22 had in mind when he spoke of the הארץ החדשה אשר אני עושה, “the new earth which I am going to make.” In that future foreseen by the prophet, the sun will once more orbit like a circle instead of like an ellipsis, a configuration responsible for the uneven temperatures on earth during different parts of the year. At that time, the whole vegetation on earth will undergo an upgrading, the curse earth experienced both after Adam’s sin and after Kayin’s murder of his brother Hevel being lifted. Conditions of length of human life will revert to what they had been before the deluge, as the prophet said that a נער, a youth, if he dies at a hundred years old will be considered as having died in his adolescence and more verses there describing this future. This is also what David referred to in Psalms 65,9 מוצאי בוקר וערב תרנין, “You make the lands of sunrise and sunset shout for joy.”

### Verse 4

###### Sforno on Genesis 9:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/9:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/9:4:1)


**אַךְ בָּשָׂר בְּנַפְשׁוֹ.** אַךְ בָּשָׂר שֶׁל חַי בְּעוֹדוֹ עִם נַפְשׁוֹ שֶׁהוּא חַי:


אך בשר בנפשו, but the flesh of living animals, while its life-force is still inside it,


###### Sforno on Genesis 9:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/9:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/9:4:2)


**דָמוֹ.** וְגַם כֵּן דָּמוֹ שֶׁפֵּרַשׁ בְּעוֹדוֹ חַי:


i.e. this life-force is equivalent to its blood; you must neither eat its flesh nor its blood,


###### Sforno on Genesis 9:4:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/9:4:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/9:4:3)


**לֹא תֹאכֵלוּ.** אֲבָל פֵּרַשׁ מִן הַמֵּת הָיָה מֻתָּר לִבְנֵי נֹחַ.


לא תאכלו, only in that state must you not eat it; once the blood has left the body, gentiles are allowed to eat blood.



### Verse 5

###### Sforno on Genesis 9:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/9:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/9:5:1)


**וְאַךְ אֶת דִּמְכֶם לְנַפְשׁוֹתֵיכֶם אֶדְרֹשׁ.** אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁלֹּא אֶדְרֹשׁ דַּם שְׁאָר בַּעֲלֵי חַיִּים מִיֶּדְכֶם, מִכָּל מָקוֹם אֶדְרֹשׁ אֶת דִּמְכֶם בִּשְׁבִיל נַפְשׁוֹתֵיכֶם שֶׁהֵן חֲשׁוּבוֹת אֶצְלִי יוֹתֵר מִנַּפְשׁוֹת כָּל שְׁאָר בַּעֲלֵי חַיִּים. וְזֹאת הַדְּרִישָׁה תִּהְיֶה עַל אוֹפַנִּים מִתְחַלְּפִים, שֶׁאִם יִזְכֶּה הָאָדָם הִנֵּה אָז "מִיַּד כָּל חַיָּה אֶדְרְשֶׁנּוּ וּמִיַּד הָאָדָם" לְהַצִּילוֹ.


ואך את דמכם לנפשותיכם אדרוש, although I will not punish you for spilling animal’s blood, I will hold you responsible for shedding the blood of fellow human beings. Human beings, all human beings, are more precious to Me than the lives of animals. This retribution for spilling the blood of human beings may on different occasions be applied in different ways: It all depends on the worthiness of the person under attack or potential attack. If, for instance, the person deserves saving, the words מיד כל חיה אדרשנו ומיד אדם are meant in the preventive sense, i.e. G’d will save such a person’s life be it under attack from animals or from human beings. If, in the eyes of G’d, the person under attack does not deserve being saved from violent death, this still did not give the animals or other human beings the right to kill him. Therefore, in such instances, ipso facto, G’d will exact retribution from the killers.


###### Sforno on Genesis 9:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/9:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/9:5:2)


**מִיַּד אִישׁ אָחִיו אֶדְרֹשׁ אֶת נֶפֶשׁ הָאָדָם.** אִם לֹא יִזְכֶּה לָזֶה שֶׁאַצִּילֵהוּ, וְיַהַרְגֵהוּ אִישׁ אָחִיו, אֶדְרֹשׁ אֶת נֶפֶשׁ הַהָרוּג מִיַּד אָחִיו הַהוֹרְגוֹ לְהִפָּרַע מִמֶּנּוּ, אֲבָל לֹא מִיַּד הַחַיָּה.


This is the meaning of the apparently redundant words מיד איש אחיו אדרוש את נפש האדם, where the word אדרוש refers to the life of a human being that has already been killed. We find the word אדרוש in both senses depending on circumstances, compare Ezekiel 34,10. The emphasis on איש את אחיו, refers to G’d exacting such retribution from humans for killing humans, seeing that this is not their nature, or should not be their nature. He will not exact retribution from an animal which killed a human being who, in G’d’s eyes, had already forfeited its right to live.



### Verse 6

###### Sforno on Genesis 9:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/9:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/9:6:1)


**בָּאָדָם דָּמוֹ יִשָּׁפֵךְ.** בְּבֵית דִּין שֶׁל מַטָּה:


באדם דמו ישפך, a reference to a human tribunal.


###### Sforno on Genesis 9:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/9:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/9:6:2)


**כִּי בְּצֶלֶם אֱלֹהִים.** וְטַעַם הֱיוֹת דַּם הָאָדָם נִדְרָשׁ וְלֹא אֶדְרֹשׁ דַּם שְׁאָר בַּעֲלֵי חַיִּים, כִּי בְּצֶלֶם אֱלֹהִים עָשָׂה בְּצֶלֶם הָעֲצָמִים הַנִּבְדָּלִים הַנִּקְרָאִים "אֱלֹהִים" (שמות כב:ח), עָשָׂה אֵיזֶה מֵהֶם אֶת הָאָדָם. כִּי אָמְנָם מֵאָז שֶׁאָמַר הָאֵל יִתְבָּרַךְ "נַעֲשֶׂה אָדָם" (בראשית א:כו), נָתַן כֹּחַ בָּעֲצָמִים הַנִּבְדָּלִים אוֹ בְּאֶחָד מֵהֶם לְהַשְׁפִּיעַ כֹּחַ שִׂכְלִי עַל כָּל נוֹשֵׂא מוּכָן, וְהוּא כָּל אֶחָד מֵהַמִּין הָאֱנוֹשִׁי. וּבִהְיוֹת הָאָדָם בְּצֶלֶם אֱלֹהִים אֲשֶׁר הוּא נַפְשׁוֹ הָאֱנוֹשִׁית אֲשֶׁר בָּהּ הוּא חַי מַשְׂכִּיל, רָאוּי שֶׁיִּהְיֶה דָּמוֹ וְנַפְשׁוֹ הַחִיּוֹנִית הַמְשָׁרְתִים לְזֶה הַצֶּלֶם נֶחְשָׁבִים וְנִדְרָשִׁים מִיַּד מְאַבְּדֵיהֶם יוֹתֵר מֵחַיֵּי כָּל שְׁאָר בַּעֲלֵי חַיִּים:


כי בצלם אלוקים, this is the reason why G’d exacts retribution for killing human beings, whereas He does not exact retribution from man for killing animals, [even though killing, say for sport is prohibited. Ed.] The animals have not been created in the image of G’d, so that their death does not deprive the earth of a divine image. Only human beings are described in the Torah, on occasion, also as אלוהים, as for instance in Exodus 22,8. 
 את האדם, from the moment G’d had said (Genesis 1,26) נעשה אדם, “let us make a human being,” He had endowed His creatures with an independent intelligence, able to defy Him either collectively or individually. It is this “divine attribute” of man that makes him sufficiently important for his Creator to demand an accounting from those who destroy that divine image by killing a human being.

**וְאַתֶּם פְּרוּ וּרְבוּ.** וְאַל תִּשְׁפְּכוּ **דַּם הָאָדָם**:


ואתם פרו ורבו, not only must you not diminish the human population on earth, but you are commanded to increase it by being fruitful and by multiplying.



### Verse 9

###### Sforno on Genesis 9:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/9:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/9:9:1)


**וַאֲנִי הִנְנִי מֵקִים אֶת בְּרִיתִי.** עַל זֶה הַתְּנַאי שֶׁלֹּא תִּשְׁפְּכוּ דָּם נָקִי אֲנִי מֵקִים אֶת בְּרִיתִי שֶׁ**לֹּא לְשַׁחֵת עוֹד הָאָרֶץ**, אֲבָל בִּשְׁפִיכַת דָּם נָקִי תִּשָּׁחֵת הָאָרֶץ, כְּאָמְרוֹ (במדבר לה:לג) "כִּי הַדָּם הוּא יַחֲנִיף אֶת הָאָרֶץ וְלָאָרֶץ לֹא יְכֻפַּר וְגוֹ'". אֲבָל עַל כָּל שְׁאָר הָעֲבֵרוֹת יִלְקֶה הַחוֹטֵא וְלֹא תִּשָּׁחֵת הָאָרֶץ.


ואני הנני מקים את בריתי, this promise is conditional on man keeping his part of the bargain, bringing murderers to justice. If, however, innocent blood will be shed in the manner it had been before the deluge, this may lead to destruction of the earth (not necessarily by water). This is what is meant in Numbers 35,33 הדם הוא יחניף את הארץ ולארץ לא יכופר לדם אשר שפך בה כי אם בדם שפכו “for blood turns the earth into a hypocrite and there will be no atonement for the land except through the blood of him who has spilled it.”

###### Sforno on Genesis 9:13:1
