###### Sforno on Genesis 31:45:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/31:45:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/31:45:1)


**וַיְרִימֶהָ מַצֵּבָה.** לְהוֹרוֹת שֶׁיִּהְיֶה הַדָּבָר יַצִּיב וְקַיָּם:


וירימה מצבה, as a symbol that this was to be something enduring.



### Verse 47

###### Sforno on Genesis 31:47:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/31:47:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/31:47:1)


**וְיַעֲקֹב קָרָא לוֹ גַּלְעֵד.** שֶׁלֹּא שִׁנָּה אֶת לְשׁוֹנוֹ:


**He therefore called its name Galeid.** He called it by the name that Yaakov had given it, but he explained it in his own way.



### Verse 48

###### Sforno on Genesis 31:48:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/31:48:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/31:48:1)


**וַיֹּאמֶר לָבָן הַגַּל הַזֶּה עֵד.** נִכְנַע לְקָרְאוֹ בִּלְשׁוֹנוֹ שֶׁל יַעֲקֹב וְאָמַר שֶׁיִּהְיֶה עֵד עַל מַה שֶׁיֹּאמַר עַתָּה:


ויאמר לבן הגל הזה עד, he humbled himself to refer to it in Hebrew, Yaakov’s language, after having named it in Aramaic first. He meant that the pile of stones would serve as a witness to what he was about to say.



### Verse 49

###### Sforno on Genesis 31:49:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/31:49:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/31:49:1)


**וְהַמִּצְפָּה.** וְכֵן יָעִיד שֵׁם הַמִּצְפָּה שֶׁהוּא מָקוֹם סָמוּךְ לָנוּ, יִהְיֶה לְעֵד מַזְכִּיר מַה שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר.


והמצפה also the name מצפה itself was to serve as a witness. This was because immediately prior Lavan had said יצף ה' ביני ובינך, “may G’d watch between me and you.”


###### Sforno on Genesis 31:49:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/31:49:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/31:49:2)


**אֲשֶׁר אָמַר.** וְזֶה שֶׁאָמַר שֶׁהַמִּצְפֶּה גַּם כֵּן תָּעִיד עַל דְּבָרָיו הוּא מִפְּנֵי שֶׁאָמַר אָז לָבָן לְיַעֲקֹב **יִצֶף ה' בֵּינִי וּבֵינֶךָ** וְהָיָה לְדַיָּן וְשׁוֹפֵט:


אשר אמר, previously.



### Verse 50

###### Sforno on Genesis 31:50:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/31:50:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/31:50:1)


**רְאֵה אֱלֹהִים עֵד.** רְאֵה שֶׁאִם תִּבְגֹּד בִּי תִּמְעוֹל בּוֹ וְהוּא יִפְרַע מִמְּךָ, כְּעִנְיַן "וּמָעֲלָה מַעַל בַּה' וְכִחֵשׁ בַּעֲמִיתוֹ" (ויקרא ה:כא), כְּאָמְרָם זִכְרוֹנָם לִבְרָכָה שֶׁהוּא מְכַחֵשׁ בַּשְּׁלִישִׁי שֶׁבֵּינֵיהֶם:


ראה אלוקים עד, look that G’d will punish you if you do not treat my daughters properly, seeing He is a witness The whole line may be understood as analogous to Leviticus 5,21, ומעלה מעל בה', where the trespass against G’d is considered as something that only G’d is aware of, there being no human witnesses at hand to testify against the sinner. G’d is always the third party when two people have a disagreement, seeing He knows the true facts of the situation.



### Verse 53

###### Sforno on Genesis 31:53:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/31:53:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/31:53:1)


**אֱלֹהֵי אֲבִיהֶם.** אָמַר לָבָן: אֵין לְךָ לְמָאֵן מַה שֶׁיִּחַדְתִּי לְדַיָּן **אֱלֹהֵי נָחוֹר** עִם **אֱלֹהֵי אַבְרָהָם**, כִּי אָמְנָם **אֱלֹהֵי** נָחוֹר הָיָה אֱלֹהָיו שֶׁל תֶּרַח שֶׁהָיָה אֲבִיהֶם שֶׁל אַבְרָם וְשֶׁל נָחוֹר:


אלוהי אביהם, Lavan meant that Yaakov could not take offence at Lavan pairing Nachor’s god and Yaakov’s G’d in a single expression. He had chosen Nachor to underline that Nachor’s god was also the god of Terach, who was the father of both Avraham and Nachor.


###### Sforno on Genesis 31:53:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/31:53:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/31:53:2)


**וַיִּשָּׁבַע יַעֲקֹב בְּפַחַד אָבִיו יִצְחָק.** שֶׁיִּצְחָק אֵינוֹ בֶּן תֶּרַח, וְנִשְׁבַּע שֶׁאֱלֹהָיו שֶׁל יִצְחָק לְבַדּוֹ יִשְׁפּוֹט אִם יִבְגּוֹד:


וישבע יעקב בפחד אביו יצחק. He chose someone who was not the son of Terach to make certain it was understood that his oath was only to the G’d of Yitzchok. Only this G’d was entitled to judge him.



### Verse 54

###### Sforno on Genesis 31:54:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/31:54:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/31:54:1)


**וַיִּקְרָא לְאֶחָיו.** שֶׁל לָבָן, אֲבָל לֹא הֻצְרַךְ לִקְרוֹא אֶת לָבָן כִּי הוּא הָיָה אָז כְּאָב לִבְנֵי יַעֲקֹב:


ויקרא לאחיו, of Lavan. There was no need to invite Lavan himself seeing he was like a father to Yaakov’s children.



## Chapter 32


### Verse 1

###### Sforno on Genesis 32:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:1:1)


**וַיְבָרֶךְ אֶתְהֶן.** כְּבָר אָמְרוּ: "אַל תְּהִי בִּרְכַּת הֶדְיוֹט קַלָּה בְּעֵינֶיךָ" (מגילה טו.). אָמְנָם סִפֵּר בִּרְכַּת לָבָן לִבְנוֹתָיו לְהוֹרוֹת שֶׁבִּרְכַּת הָאָב אֲשֶׁר הִיא עַל בָּנָיו בְּכָל נַפְשׁוֹ בְּלִי סָפֵק רָאוּי שֶׁתָּחוּל יוֹתֵר בִּסְגֻלַּת צֶלֶם אֱלֹהִים הַמְבָרֵךְ, כְּאָמְרוֹ "בַּעֲבוּר תְּבָרֶכְךָ נַפְשִׁי" (בראשית כז:ד).


ויברך אתהם. Our sages have already told us not to belittle the value of a blessing pronounced by a layman, a person of low social standing. (Megillah 15) The reason why the Torah tells us of Lavan’s blessing to his daughters is to remind us that when a father blesses his sons he does so wholeheartedly, with his material and spiritual faculties. We know that Yitzchok blessed with all his soul from his own words (27,4) When the one bestowing the blessing himself reflects the image of G’d he had been created in, then such a blessing is likely to be correspondingly more effective.



### Verse 3

###### Sforno on Genesis 32:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:3:1)


**מַחֲנֵה אֱלֹהִים זֶה.** הַמַּחֲנֶה שֶׁלִּי שֶׁפָּגְעוּ בּוֹ מַלְאֲכֵי אֱלֹהִים, אֵין סָפֵק שֶׁהוּא מַחֲנֵה אֱלֹהִים, כְּעִנְיַן "וַיִּקְרָא לַמָּקוֹם אֵל בֵּית אֵל" (בראשית כח:יט), כִּי שָׁם נִגְלוּ אֵלָיו הָאֱלֹהִים:


מחנה אלוקים זה. Now that the angels have seen fit to join me there can be no doubt that mine is a godly camp. Just as Yaakov had called the place where he had had the dream of the ladder Bet El, so he now referred to his camp as machaneh elokim. In either instance the reason was that he had been found worthy of a divine revelation.


###### Sforno on Genesis 32:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:3:2)


**מַחֲנָיִם.** שְׁתֵּי מַחֲנוֹת – אֱלֹהִים שֶׁל מַלְאָכִים וְשֶׁלּוֹ, כְּמִשְׁפַּט סִימָן הָרַבִּים הַבָּא אַחַר פַּתָּח מִלְּעֵיל, הַמּוֹרִים עַל מִסְפַּר שְׁנַיִם, כְּמוֹ פַּעֲמַיִם, שְׁבוּעַיִם, שְׁנָתַיִם וְזוּלָתָם. חסלת פרשת ויצא.


מחנים, two camps; the camp of G’d (angels) and his own. The grammatical formulation with the plural ending following the vowel patach, the stress is on the penultimate syllable as this indicated the number 2. Parallel examples are paamayim, shevuayim, shenatayim, etc. .[the kametz here substitutes for the patach as it is the end of the sentence. Ed.]



### Verse 4

###### Sforno on Genesis 32:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:4:1)


**וַיִּשְׁלַח יַעֲקֹב מַלְאָכִים.** לְדֵעָה מַה בְּלֵב אָחִיו לָדַעַת מַה יַּעֲשֶׂה לוֹ:


וישלח יעקב מלאכים, in order to find out Esau’s state of mind concerning him.


###### Sforno on Genesis 32:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:4:2)


**אַרְצָה שֵׂעִיר שְׂדֵה אֱדוֹם.** אֶל גְּלִיל שֵׂעִיר, לְאוֹתוֹ הַחֵלֶק מִמֶּנּוּ שֶׁהָיָה דָּר שָׁם אֱדוֹם, כִּי אָז לֹא כָּבַשׁ עֲדַיִן אֶת כָּל הַחוֹרִי יוֹשְׁבֵי הָאָרֶץ (בראשית לו:ח).


ארצה שעיר שדה אדום, to the region of Seir where he lived. At that time he had not yet conquered the whole Chori, the inhabitants of that land. (compare 36,8)



### Verse 5

###### Sforno on Genesis 32:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:5:1)


**וָאֵחַר עַד עָתָּה.** לְפִיכָךְ לֹא בָּאתִי לְהִשְׁתַּחֲוֹת לְךָ עַד עָתָּה.


ואחר עד עתה. This is why I did not come to pay my respects to you until now.



### Verse 6

###### Sforno on Genesis 32:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:6:1)


**לִמְצֹא חֵן בְּעֵינֶיךָ.** כִּי אֵין סָפֵק אֶצְלִי שֶׁתִּשְׂמַח עַל הֱיוֹת לִי עֹשֶׁר וְכָבוֹד, וְאֶמְצָא חֵן בְּעֵינֶיךָ כְּשֶׁאֲבַשֶּׂרְךָ בָּזֶה.


למצוא חן בעיניך, for I have no doubt that you will be happy to see that I have become wealthy and the fact that I tell you the good news will please you.



### Verse 7

###### Sforno on Genesis 32:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:7:1)


**וְגַם הוֹלֵךְ לִקְרָאתְךָ.** לֹא בִּלְבַד רָאִינוּ שֶׁלֹּא הָיְתָה בְּעֵינָיו כָּל בְּשׂוֹרָה מוּצֵאת כַּאֲשֶׁר הִגַּדְנוּ אֶת עָשְׁרְךָ, אֲבָל גַּם הוֹלֵךְ לִקְרָאתְךָ עִם אַרְבַּע מֵאוֹת אִישׁ, וְאֵין זֶה אֶלָּא לְהִלָּחֵם, כְּעִנְיָן "וַיֵּצֵא אֱדוֹם לִקְרָאתוֹ בְּעַם כָּבֵד" (במדבר כ:כ).


וגם הולך לקראתך, not only have we seen him, but he did not react with satisfaction when we told him about your wealth. But, אגם הולך לקראתך עם ארבע מאות איש, he also marches toward you with 400 men in order to attack you. The expression לקראת meaning to approach with hostile intent is repeated in Numbers 20,20 when again Edom (Esau) is threatening the Israelites.



### Verse 9

###### Sforno on Genesis 32:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:9:1)


**וְהָיָה הַמַּחֲנֶה הַנִּשְׁאָר לִפְלֵיטָה.** כִּי בְּעוֹד שֶׁיִּעַט אֶל הַשָּׁלָל הָרִאשׁוֹן יִמָּלֵט הַשֵּׁנִי בִּכְלֵי מִלְחָמָה אוֹ שֶׁיִּבְרַח:


והיה המחנה הנשאר לפלטה, while he will be busy gorging himself on the loot captured in the first camp the second camp will be able to make good its escape, or will have prepared itself for battle.



### Verse 10

###### Sforno on Genesis 32:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:10:1)


**אֱלֹהֵי אָבִי אַבְרָהָם.** הִקְדִּים בְּסֵדֶר שִׁבְחֵי הַמָּקוֹם וַחֲסָדָיו, וּבְהַזְכִּיר זְכוּת אָבוֹת, כְּסֵדֶר אַנְשֵׁי כְּנֶסֶת הַגְּדוֹלָה בִּתְחִלַּת שְׁמוֹנֶה עֶשְׂרֵה בְּרָכוֹת.


אלוקי אבי אברהם, Yaakov first listed the praises of the Lord, acknowledging His deeds of loving kindness, before coming to the point of pleading for His help in his hour of need. When the sages of the Great Assembly formulated our daily prayers they followed the example set by Yaakov here in devoting the first three benedictions to praising the Lord and acknowledging both His power and His Holiness, before launching into listing our requests from Him.



### Verse 11

###### Sforno on Genesis 32:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:11:1)


**קָטֹנְתִּי מִכֹּל הַחֲסָדִים.** לֹא הָיִיתִי רָאוּי לְכָל אוֹתָם הַחֲסָדִים:


קטונתי מכל החסדים, I was not worthy of all the acts of kindness I have experienced at Your hands.


###### Sforno on Genesis 32:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:11:2)


**וּמִכָּל הָאֱמֶת.** הַטּוֹב שֶׁ**עָשִׂיתָ** עִמָּדִי בִּזְכוּת אֲבוֹתַי, וּבְאוֹתוֹ הַדֶּרֶךְ שֶׁעָשִׂיתָ עִמָּדִי לִפְנִים מִשּׁוּרַת הַדִּין, אֲנִי מְבַקֵּשׁ שֶׁתַּצִּילֵנִי, כְּעִנְיַן אָמְרוֹ "כְּגֹדֶל חַסְדֶּךָ וְכַאֲשֶׁר נָשָׂאתָ לָעָם הַזֶּה" (במדבר יד:יט):


ומכל האמת, the good You did for me on account of the merit of my father and grandfather. Seeing that You have already seen fit to treat me well beyond my deserts, I beg of You to continue to do so. Our regular prayers reflect this sentiment when we use the expression כגודל חסדך, “in accordance with the greatness of Your kindness.” Moses used this expression in Numbers 14,19 when praying for forgiveness of the people after the debacle with the spies.



### Verse 12

###### Sforno on Genesis 32:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:12:1)


**וְהִכַּנִי אֵם עַל בָּנִים.** יַכֶּה אוֹתִי בָּזֶה שֶׁיַּהֲרֹג אֵם עַל בָּנִים, גַּם שֶׁאוּכַל אֲנִי לְהִמָּלֵט מִיָּדוֹ:


**Mother and children alike.** Literally, “he will strike me, mother with sons” — i.e. he would deal Yaakov a devastating blow by killing his family, even if he himself escaped.



### Verse 13

###### Sforno on Genesis 32:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:13:1)


**וְאַתָּה אָמַרְתָּ הֵיטֵב אֵיטִיב עִמָּךְ.** וְאִם יִקְרֶה זֶה יִהְיֶה הֵפֶךְ מַה שֶּׁאָמַרְתָּ לְהֵיטִיב עִמִּי, אִם אֶרְאֶה בְּאָבְדַן מוֹלַדְתִּי.


ואתה אמרת היטב איטיב עמך, if what I am afraid of were to happen this would be the reverse of Your promise to me. How can I watch the destruction of my family?


###### Sforno on Genesis 32:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:13:2)


**וְשַׂמְתִּי אֶת זַרְעֲךָ.** וְהִנֵּה הֵפֶךְ זֶה גַּם כֵּן יִהְיֶה אִם יַכְרִית זַרְעִי, וְרָאוּי שֶׁתַּעֲשֶׂה עִמָּם בַּעֲבוּר כְּבוֹד שְׁמֶךָ לְקַיֵּם הַבְטָחָתְךָ, אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁאֵינִי רָאוּי לָהּ, כְּעִנְיַן "אִם עֲוֹנֵינוּ עָנוּ בָנוּ ה' עֲשֵׂה לְמַעַן שְׁמֶךָ" (ירמיהו יד:ז).


ושמתי את זרעך, even if only my children will be killed this will be the opposite of what You promised. It behooves You to let them survive for the sake of Your great name, even if I myself do not deserve to survive. We use this kind of argument in our prayers when we say (based on Jeremiah 14,7) אם עונינו ענו בנו עשה למען שמך, “if our sins testify against us, (save us) for the sake of Your name!”



### Verse 17

###### Sforno on Genesis 32:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:17:1)


**עֵדֶר עֵדֶר לְבַדּוֹ.** כְּדֵי שֶׁיִּרְאֶה שֶׁיֵּשׁ בְּכָל מִין מִנְיָן זְכָרִים וּנְקֵבוֹת כְּפִי הָרָאוּי לִמְלֶאכֶת הַמִּקְנֶה, שֶׁיּוּכַל לִפְרוֹת וְלִרְבּוֹת, כְּאָמְרוֹ "קַח נָא אֶת בִּרְכָתִי" (בראשית לג:יא), שֶׁהָיְתָה מִנְחָה מוּכֶנֶת לִבְרָכָה שֶׁהִיא תּוֹסֶפֶת טוֹבָה.


עדר עדר לבדו. In order for Esau to realise that each species of animals had the appropriate number of males with it to ensure that these herds would continue to develop without hindrance. This is what he meant when he urged Esau קח נא את ברכתי, “please accept my blessing” (33,11) He meant that his gift was designed to be an ongoing blessing for his brother.


###### Sforno on Genesis 32:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:17:2)


**וְרֶוַח תָּשִׂימוּ בֵּין עֵדֶר וּבֵין עֵדֶר.** שֶׁלֹּא יִקְפְּצוּ הַבְּהֵמוֹת מֵעֵדֶר לְעֵדֶר וְיִתְעָרְבוּ, וְלֹא יָבִין הָרוֹאֶה חָכְמַת הַמִּנְחָה וּבִרְכָתָהּ.


וריוח תשימו בין עדר לעדר, to prevent the animals form one herd to jump into the adjoining herd, so that anyone seeing this gift would not appreciate its quality and scope.



### Verse 19

###### Sforno on Genesis 32:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:19:1)


**וְאָמַרְתָּ לְעַבְדְּךָ לְיַעֲקֹב.** צִוָּה שֶׁלֹּא יִרְאֶה הַשָּׁלִיחַ אֶת עַצְמוֹ כְּמַכִּיר אֶת עֵשָׂו וְשָׁלוּחַ אֵלָיו, פֶּן יַחְשֹׁב עֵשָׂו שֶׁיָּדַע אָחִיו שֶׁהוּא הָלַךְ לִקְרָאתוֹ וְשָׁלַח לוֹ דּוֹרוֹן מֵחֲמַת יִרְאָה, אֲבָל יִרְאֶה הַשָּׁלִיחַ אֶת עַצְמוֹ כְּמוֹ שָׁלוּחַ לְשֵׂעִיר וּכְבִלְתִּי מַכִּיר אֶת עֵשָׂו.


ואמרת: "לעבדך ליעקב". He instructed his shepherds not to give the impression that they knew Esau was on the way in their direction and that they had been sent to him. The phrasing Yaakov instructed his messengers to use was meant to give the impression that Yaakov had no knowledge that Esau was marching toward him with hostile intentions. The messenger should give the impression that he had been sent by Yaakov to Seir where Esau lived, with a gift for him.



### Verse 20

###### Sforno on Genesis 32:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:20:1)


**כַּדָּבָר הַזֶּה תְּדַבְּרוּן אֶל עֵשָׂו.** לְכָל אֶחָד צִוָּה מַה יָּשִׁיב לְעֵשָׂו, וְיוֹדִיעַ שֶׁהוּא עִם שְׁאָר כָּל הָעֲדָרִים, וְהֵם כֻּלָּם שֶׁל יַעֲקֹב **מִנְחָה לְעֵשָׂו אָחִיו** הַגָּדוֹל:


כדבר הזה תדברון אל עשו, he instructed each one what to reply to Esau’s enquiry. He should simply mention that he belonged to the other herds following him, all of them being Yaakov’s a gift for his older brother Esau.



### Verse 21

###### Sforno on Genesis 32:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:21:1)


**וַאֲמַרְתֶּם גַּם הִנֵּה עַבְדְּךָ יַעֲקֹב אַחֲרֵינוּ.** צִוָּה שֶׁכָּל אֶחָד מֵהֶם יֹאמַר זֶה הַדִּבּוּר מִלְּבַד הַדִּבּוּר הַמְיֻחָד לוֹ, כְּדֵי שֶׁיִּמְצָא עֵשָׂו דִּבְרֵי כֻלָּם מְכֻוָּנִים בָּזֶה שֶׁהָיָה יַעֲקֹב הוֹלֵךְ אַחֲרֵיהֶם לְשֵׂעִיר לְהַקְבִּיל פְּנֵי אָחִיו.


ואמרתם גם הנה עבדך יעקב אחרינו, he told each of them to reply with the same words in addition to the words that each one had been instructed to answer individually. The purpose behind these detailed instructions was to convince Esau that Yaakov was indeed not far behind on his way to Seir the destination of these gifts which he had sent ahead of him.


###### Sforno on Genesis 32:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:21:2)


**כִּי אָמַר אֲכַפְּרָה פָנָיו בַּמִּנְחָה.** וְאֵלֶּה הַדְּבָרִים שָׂם בְּפִי עֲבָדָיו, כְּדֵי שֶׁיְּמַעֵט רָגְזוֹ שֶׁל עֵשָׂו בְּדִבּוּר הַהַכְנָעָה עִם הַמִּנְחָה.


כי אמר אכפרה פניו במנחה, these were the words he had instructed the shepherds leading the gift to say, in order to soften Esau’s attitude by Yaakov assuming a somewhat servile attitude prior to their coming face to face with one another.


###### Sforno on Genesis 32:21:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:21:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:21:3)


**אֶרְאֶה פָנָיו.** דֶּרֶךְ הַבִּקּוּר הָרָאוּי לְשָׂרִים, כְּעִנְיַן "יֵרָאֶה כָל זְכוּרְךָ אֶת פְּנֵי הָאָדוֹן" (שמות כג:יז), "וְלֹא יֵרָאוּ פָנַי רֵיקָם". וְכֵן אָמַר לְעֵשָׂו אַחַר כָּךְ "כִּי עַל כֵּן רָאִיתִי פָנֶיךָ כִּרְאוֹת פְּנֵי אֱלֹהִים" (בראשית לג:י), כִּי הַמִּנְהָג לִפְקוֹד אֶת הַשָּׂרִים בְּמִנְחָה עִם רְאִיַּת פְּנֵיהֶם.


אראה פניו, an expression denoting the “paying of a visit,” as we know from Exodus 34,23 where the Jewish people is instructed to visit the Temple three times annually with the same formula, i.e. יראה כל זכורך...ולא יראו פני ריקם, “every male of you is to visit Me; when they pay this visit they shall not come empty-handed.” Yaakov himself confirmed the meaning of the formula אראה פניך when he said to Esau in 33,10 על כן ראיתי פניך כראות פני אלוקים, “inasmuch as visiting you is akin to visiting the presence of G’d.” It was an accepted custom for a visitor to highly placed persons to arrive bearing gifts.



### Verse 22

###### Sforno on Genesis 32:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:22:1)


**וַתַּעֲבוֹר הַמִּנְחָה עַל פָּנָיו.** לִרְאוֹת שֶׁתִּהְיֶה עַל סֵדֶר רָאוּי לְתַכְלִית הַמְכֻוָּן מֵאִתּוֹ.


ותעבור המנחה על פניו, Yaakov rehearsed the proceedings in order to satisfy himself that it would accomplish to give the impression he had meant for it to convey.



### Verse 24

###### Sforno on Genesis 32:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:24:1)


**וַיַּעֲבֵר אֶת אֲשֶׁר לוֹ.** צִוָּה לְכֻלָּם שֶׁיַּקְדִּימוּ לָלֶכֶת לְפָנָיו לְהָלְאָה מִן הַנַּחַל, כְּמוֹ "וַיַּעֲבֹר אֶת הַכּוּשִׁי" (שמואל ב יח:כג).


ויעבר את אשר לו, he ordered all of them to precede him in crossing the river; we have a similar construction in Samuel II 18,23 ויעבור את הכושי, “he passed the Cushite.”



### Verse 25

###### Sforno on Genesis 32:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:25:1)


**וַיִּוָּתֵר יַעֲקֹב לְבַדּוֹ.** אַחֲרוֹן לְכֻלָּם לִנְסֹעַ מִן הַמַּחֲנֶה, כְּדֵי לְהַדְרִיךְ אֶת כָּל עַמּוֹ שֶׁיֵּלְכוּ עִם כָּל קִנְיָנוֹ וְלֹא יִשָּׁאֵר דָּבָר בַּמַּחֲנֶה:


ויותר יעקב לבדו, he was the last one to leave from the camp as he was busy directing all his family and servants and chattels to cross the fording so that nothing would be left behind.


###### Sforno on Genesis 32:25:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:25:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:25:2)


**וַיֵּאָבֵק אִישׁ עִמּוֹ.** הָיָה זֶה פֹּעַל מַלְאָךְ בְּמִצְוַת בּוֹרְאוֹ בְּלִי סָפֵק, עַל דֶּרֶךְ "יְרֵה וַיּוֹר" (מלכים ב יג:יז), שֶׁיִּהְיֶה נוֹשַׁע בַּה' יַעֲקֹב וְזַרְעוֹ, אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁיִּהְיֶה עִם הֶזֵּק בְּמָמוֹן שֶׁהוּא הַיְקוּם אֲשֶׁר בְּרַגְלֵיהֶם, וְעִם זֶה תִּהְיֶה הַתְּשׁוּעָה בַּסּוֹף עִם בְּרָכָה.


ויאבק איש עמו, this was the work of an angel at the instigation of G’d (that is why he is called איש). The description is parallel to Kings II 13,17 It is immaterial whether the instrument of the salvation is a merely symbolic one such as the arrows described in the Book of Kings which Elisha instructed King Yoash to shoot in the direction of the far away Kingdom of Aram. Salvation occurs at the end of a period of ups and downs, and though Yaakov/Israel suffered many reverses in his struggle with Esau, (in the historical global struggle between the two philosophies) in the end Yaakov triumphs. [freely translated as the author is ambiguous to my mind. Ed.] The blessing given by the angel here to Yaakov at the end of the struggle, at daybreak, symbolises the synopsis of Jewish history.



### Verse 26

###### Sforno on Genesis 32:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:26:1)


**לֹא יָכוֹל לוֹ.** לְרֹב דְּבֵקוּתוֹ תָּמִיד בָּאֵל יִתְבָּרַךְ בְּמַחֲשָׁבָה וּבְדִבּוּר:


לא יכול לו, seeing that most of Yaakov’s striving was oriented toward G’d and heavenly concerns. Both his thinking and his conversation had G’d and His will as its focus.


###### Sforno on Genesis 32:26:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:26:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:26:2)


**וַיִּגַּע.** הוֹדִיעוֹ הַחֵטְא הֶעָתִיד בְּמַדְרִיכֵי עַמּוֹ, וּבְדַאֲגָתוֹ בָּזֶה פָּסַק הַדִּבּוּר, **וַתֵּקַע כַּף** יְרֵכוֹ **בְּהֵאָבְקוֹ**:


ויגע he informed him that in the future, i.e. כף ירכו, many of his offspring would become guilty of sins against G’d. While this troubled Yaakov he momentarily digressed from his concentration on G’d so that the angel could inflict an injury upon him during his lapse of concentration.



### Verse 27

###### Sforno on Genesis 32:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:27:1)


**לֹא אֲשַׁלֵּחֲךָ.** שֶׁגְּדוֹלִים צַדִּיקִים יוֹתֵר מִמַּלְאֲכֵי הַשָּׁרֵת (סנהדרין צב:).


לא אשלחך, this utterance by Yaakov confirms what the sages tell us that the righteous are greater than the ministering angels (Sanhedrin 92).



### Verse 29

###### Sforno on Genesis 32:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:29:1)


**לֹא יַעֲקֹב יֵאָמֵר עוֹד שִׁמְךָ.** לְקֵץ הַיָּמִין שֶׁתִּהְיֶה קַיָּם אַחַר אָבְדַן הָאֻמּוֹת, וּבְכֵן יִהְיֶה כְּבָר מְקֻיָּם בְּךָ עִנְיַן **יַעֲקֹב**, הַמּוֹרֶה קַיָּם בַּסּוֹף, וְלָכֵן לֹא יִפּוֹל עָלֶיךָ אָז עוֹד שֵׁם **יַעֲקֹב** לְהוֹרוֹת שֶׁתִּהְיֶה בֶּעָקֵב וּבְסוֹף כָּל הָאֻמּוֹת.


לא יעקב יאמר עוד שמך, a reference to the end of days when Israel will have survived the destruction of the gentile nations When that time comes no one ever will again use the name Yaakov for the Jewish people [and the stigma that used to be associated with that name. Ed.] The very word יעקב already contained within this message that the bearer of this name will triumph at the end. Once he has triumphed there is no more point in having a name which alludes to something which will be realised only in the future. The future will then have arrived!


###### Sforno on Genesis 32:29:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:29:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:29:2)


**כִּי אִם יִשְׂרָאֵל כִּי שָׂרִיתָ.** אֲבָל יִקְרְאוּ לְךָ בְּשֵׁם יִשְׂרָאֵל בִּלְבָד, לְהוֹרוֹת שֶׁאָז כְּבָר שָׂרִיתָ **עִם אֱלֹהִים כּוּ'**, כְּעִנְיַן (ישעיהו כד:כא) "יִפְקֹד ה' עַל צְבָא הַמָּרוֹם בַּמָּרוֹם וְעַל מַלְכֵי הָאֲדָמָה עַל הָאֲדָמָה":


כי אם ישראל כי שרית, they will only refer to as “Israel.” This new name is to confirm that שרית עם אלוקים ועם אנשים, this is analogous to Isaiah 24,21 יפקוד ה' על צבא המרום במרום ועל מלכי האדמה על האדמה, “in that day the Lord will punish the host of heaven in heaven and the kings of the earth on earth.”



### Verse 30

###### Sforno on Genesis 32:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:30:1)


**הַגִּידָה נָּא שְׁמֶךָ.** הַמּוֹרֶה עַל צוּרָתְךָ וְעַל הַפֹּעַל הַנִּמְשָׁךְ מִמֶּנּוּ, כְּדֵי שֶׁאֶתְבּוֹנֵן עַל מָה קַמְתָּ לְשָׂטָן, וְאָשׁוּב בִּתְשׁוּבָה וְאֶתְפַּלֵּל.


הגידה נא שמך, which would describe your essence, your function, and how you would go about performing same. This would enable me to understand why you attacked me in the first place. I would then be able to do penitence for my sin, something I cannot do as long as I do not know what precisely my sin consists of.


###### Sforno on Genesis 32:30:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:30:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:30:2)


**לָמָּה זֶּה תִּשְׁאַל לִשְׁמִי.** כִּי הַצּוּרָה הָאִישִׁית לָנוּ הִיא מַדְרֵגַת הַשְׂכָּלָתֵנוּ אֲשֶׁר לֹא תְבֹאַר בְּשׁוּם דִּבּוּר, כְּאָמְרוֹ (שופטים יג:יח) "וְהוּא פֶלִאי", וְהַפֹּעַל שֶׁלָּהּ הוּא כְּפִי הָרָצוֹן הָאֱלֹהִי.


?למה זה תשאל לשמי, our (angels) format does not in any way correspond and shed light on the level of our intelligence which cannot be explained in terms of language used by human beings. The angel confronting Manoach in Judges 13,18 summed this up in the single word: “פלאי,” it is concealed, [because it would only be misunderstood if put into words. Ed.] The angel’s activity reflects G’d’s will at the time. [Clearly, this implies that most angels perform their tasks ad hoc. Ed.]



### Verse 32

###### Sforno on Genesis 32:32:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:32:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:32:1)


**וַיִּזְרַח לוֹ הַשֶּׁמֶשׁ וְכוּ'.** אַחַר שֶׁעָבַר אֶת פְּנוּאֵל בִּצְלִיעָה, זָרַח הַשֶּׁמֶשׁ וְנִרְפָּא, כַּאֲשֶׁר יִהְיֶה לֶעָתִיד לָבֹא, כְּאָמְרוֹ "וְזָרְחָה לָכֶם יִרְאֵי שְׁמִי שֶׁמֶשׁ צְדָקָה וּמַרְפֵּא" (מלאכי ג:כ).


**The sun shone upon him.** This verse must be inverted — after he passed Penuel, still limping, the sun shone and healed him. Similarly, in the time to come the “sun” of the redemption will shine and heal the righteous.



### Verse 33

###### Sforno on Genesis 32:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/32:33:1)


**עַל כֵּן לֹא יֹאכְלוּ בְנֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל.** כְּדֵי שֶׁיִּהְיֶה הַהֶיזֵק אֲשֶׁר הוּרַע בִּנְגִיעַת כַּף הַיָּרֵךְ, הֶיזֵק בְּדָבָר בִּלְתִּי נֶחְשָׁב אֶצְלֵנוּ.


על כן לא יאכלו בני ישראל, in order to ensure that the damage inflicted to Yaakov’s offspring as predicted by the angel should be kept to a minimum as long as they remind themselves of the symbolic meaning of that injury suffered by Yaakov during his nocturnal encounter with this angel. [I believe that even commentators who concentrate on the plain meaning of the text, such the ones whose commentaries I am translating in these volumes, feel free to treat the text allegorically when the narrative of the Torah itself appears pointless unless we understand it in historical terms, offering moral and ethical teachings and warnings of long term significance. Ed.] Alternatively, the moral lesson is that just as the gid hanashe is totally devoid of taste, does not appeal to the palate, so that consuming it would not provide the sinner with any kind of gratification, we are taught not to second-guess G’d’s legislation by treating this commandment as not important.



## Chapter 33


### Verse 1

###### Sforno on Genesis 33:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:1:1)


**וְעִמּוֹ אַרְבַּע מֵאוֹת אִישׁ.** שֶׁלֹּא הִתְכַּפְּרוּ פָּנָיו בַּמִּנְחָה.


ועמו ארבע מרות איש. To tell us that he had not been appeased even by the substantial gift Yaakov had sent him.



### Verse 4

###### Sforno on Genesis 33:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:4:1)


**וַיָּרָץ עֵשָׂו.** נֶהְפַּךְ לִבּוֹ כְּמוֹ רֶגַע בְּהַכְנָעוֹתָיו שֶׁל יַעֲקֹב, כְּעִנְיָנֵנוּ בַּגָּלוּת עִם בְּנֵי עֵשָׂו הָאוֹמֵר בְּגָבְהוֹ "מִי יוֹרִידֵנוּ אָרֶץ", וְהוֹרָה שֶׁנִּהְיֶה נִמְלָטִים מִיַּד חֶרֶב גַּאֲוָתוֹ בְּהַכְנָעָה וּמִנְחָה, כְּאָמְרָם זִכְרוֹנָם לִבְרָכָה (תענית כ.) שֶׁאֲחִיָּהוּ הַשִּׁילוֹנִי קִלֵּל אֶת יִשְׂרָאֵל בְּקָנֶה הַנִּכְנָע לְכָל הָרוּחוֹת (מלכים א יד:טו). הֵן לוּ עָשׂוּ כֵן בִּרְיוֹנֵי בַּיִת שֵׁנִי, לֹא הָיָה נֶחֱרַב בֵּית מִקְדָּשֵׁנוּ, כְּמוֹ שֶׁהֵעִיד רַבִּי יוֹחָנָן בֶּן זַכַּאי בְּאָמְרוֹ (גיטין נו.) "בִּרְיוֹנֵי דְבָן לָא שָׁבְקִינָן".


וירץ עשו, his attitude changed suddenly when he realised to what extent Yaakov had humbled himself before him. It is of great concern to us seeing that we live among the descendants of Esau, people who are arrogant, consider themselves invincible. Yaakov’s conduct vis a vis Esau teaches that the only way to escape the sword of Esau is through self degradation and gifts. This corresponds to what our sages taught us (Taanit 20) that the curses of the prophet Achiyah Hashiloni were more beneficial to the Jewish people than the blessing showered upon them by Bileam. Achiyah had cursed the Jewish people describing them as a reed which bends in all directions, a reference to someone demeaning himself rather than facing up to adversaries. (Kings I 14,15) If the Jewish people during the period of the second Temple had taken his advice to heart, the Temple would not have been destroyed. Rabbi Yochanan ben Zakkai testified to this when he said “if not for the militant extremists the Romans would not have destroyed the Temple.”



### Verse 5

###### Sforno on Genesis 33:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:5:1)


**מִי אֵלֶּה לָּךְ.** שָׁאַל אִם הֵם בָּנָיו אוֹ עֲבָדָיו וּבְנֵי בֵיתוֹ.


**Who are these to you.** Are they your children or your servants?.



### Verse 8

###### Sforno on Genesis 33:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:8:1)


**מִי לְךָ כָּל הַמַּחֲנֶה?** הַמַּחֲנֶה שֶׁאָמַר אֵלַי "מִנְחָה הִיא שְׁלוּחָה לַאדֹנִי" (בראשית לב:יט), מִי הוּא אֶצְלְךָ? אִם הוּא לְמִנְחַת כָּבוֹד אוֹ לְמִנְחַת חֶסֶד שֶׁחָשַׁבְתָּ שֶׁאֶצְטָרֵךְ?


**Who are these to you.** Are they your children or your servants?



### Verse 9

###### Sforno on Genesis 33:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:9:1)


**יֶשׁ לִי רָב.** וְאֵינִי צָרִיךְ:


יש לי רב, I most certainly do not need this gift.


###### Sforno on Genesis 33:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:9:2)


**אָחִי, יְהִי לְךָ אֲשֶׁר לָךְ.** שֶׁבִּהְיוֹתְךָ אָחִי לֹא תִצְטָרֵךְ לְכַבְּדֵנִי בְּמִנְחָה.


אחי, יהי לך אשר לך. Seeing that you are my brother you certainly do not have to put yourself out so much in order for me to make you welcome.



### Verse 10

###### Sforno on Genesis 33:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:10:1)


**כִּרְאוֹת פְּנֵי אֱלֹהִים.** כְּמִנְהָג בִּפְקוֹד אֶת הַשָּׂרִים, כְּאָמְרוֹ "וְלֹא יֵרָאוּ פָנַי רֵיקָם" (שמות כג:טו).


כראות פני אלוקים, as is the custom when one is granted an audience by the people in high office. The Torah already commanded that when appearing in Jerusalem three times a year, the Israelite fulfilling this commandment must not appear before G’d empty-handed. (Exodus 23,15)


###### Sforno on Genesis 33:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:10:2)


**וַתִּרְצֵנִי.** שֶׁקִּבַּלְתַּנִי בְּסֵבֶר פָּנִים יָפוֹת, כְּעִנְיַן "הֲיִרְצְךָ אוֹ הֲיִשָּׂא פָנֶיךָ" (מלאכי א:ח), וּמִצַּד זֶה גַּם כֵּן רָאוּי שֶׁאֲקַדֵּם פָּנֶיךָ בַּמִּנְחָה.


ותרצני, in order that you will receive me with goodwill. We have a similar construction in Maleachi 1,8 הירצך או הישא פניך?, This consideration also warrants my sending you a lavish gift.



### Verse 11

###### Sforno on Genesis 33:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:11:1)


**וַיִּפְצַר בּוֹ.** כְּדֵי שֶׁיְּקַבֵּל, כִּי הַשֹּׁחַד יְעַוֵּר וְיִקַּח, וְהַהֵפֶךְ בֶּאֱלִישָׁע כְּאָמוּר "וַיִּפְצַר בּוֹ לָקַחַת וַיְמָאֵן" (מלכים ב ה:טז).


ויפצר בו, in order for him to accept the gift. Yaakov was aware that bribes sway people’s minds; in this case he used the bribe to blind Esau’s judgment. We encounter the very opposite with the prophet Elisha in Kings II 5,16 where he is urged to accept a well deserved gift and refuses, so that Naaman will know that a true servant of the Lord does not accept a reward for someone else’s accomplishment, i.e. G’d having cured Naaman from his leprosy.



### Verse 12

###### Sforno on Genesis 33:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:12:1)


**נִסְעָה וְנֵלֵכָה.** לְשֵׂעִיר, כְּמוֹ שֶׁהוֹרוּ נוֹשְׂאֵי הַמִּנְחָה בְּאָמְרָם **גַּם הִנֵּה עַבְדְּךָ יַעֲקֹב אַחֲרֵינוּ**:


נסעה ונלכה, to Seir, Esau’s home. This was in line with what Yaakov’s messengers had told Esau as being their destination.



### Verse 13

###### Sforno on Genesis 33:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:13:1)


**כִּי הַיְלָדִים רַכִּים.** כִּי עָלַי מֻטָּל דְּבַר הַיְלָדִים רַכִּים **וְהַצֹּאן וְהַבָּקָר עָלוֹת**:


כי הילדים רכים, for it is my task to take care both of the children of tender age and of the sheep and other animals which are sensitive to overexertion.

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:13:2)


**וּדְפָקוּם.** לִכְבוֹדְךָ שֶׁלֹּא לְעַכֶּבְךָ בַּדֶּרֶךְ.


ודפקום, in your honour, not to delay you.



### Verse 18

###### Sforno on Genesis 33:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:18:1)


**וַיָּבֹא יַעֲקֹב שָׁלֵם עִיר שְׁכֶם.** תֵּכֶף שֶׁהִגִּיעַ בְּשָׁלוֹם בְּאֶרֶץ כְּנַעַן, כְּמוֹ שֶׁאָמַר (בראשית כח:כא) "וְשַׁבְתִּי בְשָׁלוֹם", לֹא הִמְתִּין שֶׁיִּהְיֶה בְּבֵית אָבִיו.


ויבא יעקב שלם עיר שכם, he reached the land of Israel unharmed. This was the fulfillment of the condition he had set in his vow in 28,21 ושבתי בשלום אל בית אבי, “and I will return in peace to the house of my father.” He did not wait until he had physically returned to the house of his father.


###### Sforno on Genesis 33:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:18:2)


**בְּבֹאוֹ מִפַּדַּן אֲרָם.** בְּעוֹד שֶׁהָיָה בַּדֶּרֶךְ לָלֶכֶת לְבֵית אָבִיו, אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁעֲדַיִן לֹא הִגִּיעַ שָׁם, הִתְחִיל לְקַיֵּם נִדְרוֹ וּבָנָה מִזְבֵּחַ כְּאָמְרוֹ "וְהָיָה ה' לִי לֵאלֹהִים".


בבאו מפדן ארם, while he was still on the way to his father’s house. He began to fulfill the terms of his vow by building an altar This was in accordance with his promises והיה ה' לי לאלוקים. (the last words of his vow).



### Verse 19

###### Sforno on Genesis 33:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:19:1)


**וַיִּקֶן אֶת חֶלְקַת הַשָּׂדֶה וַיַּצֶּב שָׁם מִזְבֵּחַ.** כְּאָמְרוֹ "אֵיךְ נָשִׁיר אֶת שִׁיר ה' כּוּ'" (תהלים קלז:ד):


ויקן את חלקת השדה, ויצב שם מזבח. A reminder of Psalms 137,4 “how can we sing the Lord’s songs on alien soil?”



### Verse 20

###### Sforno on Genesis 33:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/33:20:1)


**וַיִּקְרָא לוֹ אֵל אֱלֹהֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל.** קָרָא לוֹ בִּתְפִלָּתוֹ אֶת הָאֵל יִתְבָּרַךְ לֵאלֹהִים, כְּמוֹ שֶׁנִּרְאֶה בְּאָמְרוֹ "וְהָיָה ה' לִי לֵאלֹהִים" (בראשית כח:כא), וְזֶה כְּשֶׁיִּהְיֶה הוּא יִשְׂרָאֵל, שֶׁלֹּא יוּכְלוּ אוּמּוֹת הָעוֹלָם לְהַעֲבִירוֹ עַל דַּעְתּוֹ וְעַל דַּעַת קוֹנוֹ, כְּמוֹ שֶׁהִזְכִּיר בְּנִדְרוֹ לְמַעְלָה (בראשית כח:כ-כב).


ויקרא לו א-ל אלו-הי ישראל. When he prayed he referred to G’d as the G’d Who is the אלו-הי ישראל, corresponding to his vow that והיה ה' לי לאלו-הים, “G’d the merciful will be accepted by me as the G’d of Justice.” This standard of being judged by the attribute of Justice commenced only at the time when Yaakov had qualified for this level of righteousness having been given the additional name of Yisrael.



## Chapter 34


### Verse 3

###### Sforno on Genesis 34:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/34:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/34:3:1)


**וַתִּדְבַּק נַפְשׁוֹ.** עַל הֵפֶךְ בְּאַמְנוֹן לְתָמָר אַחַר שֶׁשָּׁכַב אוֹתָהּ (שמואל ב יג:יד-טז).


ותדבק נפשו, this was the opposite of Amnon having raped Tamar whose infatuation with her turned to disgust the moment he had satisfied his biological urge. (Samuel II 13,14-16)


###### Sforno on Genesis 34:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/34:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/34:3:2)


**בְּדִינָה בַּת יַעֲקֹב.** בִּשְׁבִיל שֶׁהָיְתָה בַּת יַעֲקֹב הַנִּכְבָּד בְּעֵינֵי הָאֻמּוֹת, כְּאָמְרוֹ אַחַר כָּךְ **כִּי חָפֵץ בְּבַת יַעֲקֹב**.


בדינה בת יעקב, because she was the daughter of the widely respected Yaakov, someone of an international reputation. The Torah confirms this once more later in verse 19 when it describes Shechem as desiring the “daughter of Yaakov.”



### Verse 5

###### Sforno on Genesis 34:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/34:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/34:5:1)


**וְהֶחֱרִישׁ יַעֲקֹב עַד בֹּאָם.** הֶחֱרִישׁ מִלָּצֵאת לָרִיב עַד שֶׁיָּבֹאוּ בָּנָיו וְיֵדְעוּ הַדָּבָר וְיִשְׁמְרוּ עַצְמָם מֵאַנְשֵׁי רִיבָם:


והחריש יעקב עד בואם, he refrained from starting a quarrel until his sons would have been informed of what happened so that they could be on their guard against their adversaries.



### Verse 6

###### Sforno on Genesis 34:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/34:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/34:6:1)


**וַיֵּצֵא חֲמוֹר.** כְּשֶׁרָאָה שֶׁהֶחֱרִישׁ, וְדָאַג פֶּן יַחְרֹשׁ עָלָיו רָעָה.


ויצא חמור, when he noticed that Yaakov’s reaction had been silence he began to worry about reprisals.



### Verse 7

###### Sforno on Genesis 34:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/34:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/34:7:1)


**וַיִּתְעַצְּבוּ הָאֲנָשִׁים וַיִּחַר.** הִנֵּה הָעִצָּבוֹן הָיָה עַל **כִּי נְבָלָה עָשָׂה בְּיִשְׂרָאֵל**, שֶׁהָיָה בִּזָּיוֹן אֵצֶל יִשְׂרָאֵל אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁאֵצֶל הַכְּנַעֲנִים לֹא הָיָה כֵּן, וּבִשְׁבִיל שֶׁהָיָה בִּלְתִּי מֻרְגָּל אָז לֶאֱנֹס אֶת בְּתוּלוֹת גְּדוֹלֵי הַדּוֹר, כְּאָמְרוֹ **וְכֵן לֹא יֵעָשֶׂה**, לְפִיכָךְ חָרָה לָהֶם עַל שֶׁנַּעֲשְׂתָה כָּזֹאת נֶגְדָּם.


ויתיצבו האנשים ויחר, the sadness and sorrow described by the Torah as the brothers’ reaction pertained to the despicable deed that had been perpetrated against a member of Yisrael’s family. This was in spite of the fact that whereas Jews do not rape unmarried virgins, gentiles are known to be guilty of this. Seeing that even in gentile circles it was taboo to rape the daughters of prominent citizens, the Torah adds the words וכן לא יעשה, that such a thing was totally unacceptable. Their anger, ויחר, therefore was turned against the perpetrators and against those who had condoned the act.



### Verse 10

###### Sforno on Genesis 34:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/34:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/34:10:1)


**וּסְחָרוּהָ.** עִם הֱיוֹת הַסְּחוֹרָה אָז בִּלְתִּי מֻתֶּרֶת לְגֵרִים, כְּאָמְרָם (בבא בתרא כא:): "בַּר מָתָא אַבַּר מָתָא אַחֲרִיתִי מָצֵי מְעַכֵּב:"


וסחרוה,. Event though normally, newcomers were not allowed to engage in the kind of commerce or artisanship which would compete with that practiced by the local inhabitants. We have this principle in Baba Batra 21, where it is spelled out as the right of the local inhabitant who feels economically threatened by competition from a new immigrant. He is accorded the right to lodge a complaint against the newcomer practicing his trade in his town. [an ancient form of the policy of “closed shop.” Ed.]

**הַרְבּוּ עָלַי מְאֹד.** כְּמֵשִׁיב לָכֶם אָשָׁם עַל מַה שֶׁחָטָאתִי לָכֶם.


הרבה עלי מאד, as a penance he would be prepared to pay over and above the customary amount of a dowry.



### Verse 13

###### Sforno on Genesis 34:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/34:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/34:13:1)


**בְּמִרְמָה.** שֶׁאָלוּ שֶׁיִּמּוֹלוּ בְּחָשְׁבָם שֶׁלֹּא יְקַבְּלוּ עֲלֵיהֶם אוֹ שֶׁלֹּא יוּכְלוּ לְפַתּוֹת אַנְשֵׁי עִירָם לָזֶה:


במרמה, they demanded that the people circumcise themselves expecting them to refuse. Alternatively, they thought that Shechem and Chamor, although prepared themselves to circumcise themselves, would not be able to convince the townsfolk to follow their example.


###### Sforno on Genesis 34:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/34:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/34:13:2)


**וַיְדַבֵּרוּ אֲשֶׁר טִמֵּא.** הֵשִׁיבוּ שֶׁמִּינֵי הַנְּדִיבוּת שֶׁאָמְרוּ שְׁכֶם וְאָבִיו לַעֲשׂוֹת, לֹא הָיוּ רְאוּיִים עַתָּה אַחֲרֵי שֶׁטִּמֵּא, כְּדֶרֶךְ אֶתְנַן זוֹנָה.


וידברו, אשר טמא; they said that the kind of ipso facto voluntary circumcision which Shechem and his father were willing to perform on themselves was not relevant after Shechem had already defiled their sister. They considered this as in the category of אתנן זונה, offering the price paid to a whore as a sacrifice on G’d’s altar.



### Verse 14

###### Sforno on Genesis 34:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/34:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sforno%20on%20Genesis/r/34:14:1)


**כִּי חֶרְפָּה הִיא לָנוּ.** שֶׁיִּהְיֶה נִרְאֶה שֶׁאֵין אִישׁ הָגוּן בֵּין הַמּוּלִים לְהִתְחַתֵּן עִמּוֹ:


כי חרפה היא לנו, it would appear that none of the circumcised men among us were fit to marry our sister.

**וְלָקַחְנוּ אֶת בִּתֵּנוּ.** אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁהִיא עַתָּה בְּבֵיתְכֶם, נִקַּח אוֹתָהּ מֵעִמָּכֶם:


ולקחנו את בתנו, even though she is at this point in time locked up in your house we will liberate her from you.
